  421: \label{thm:exact-negative-pressure}
  422: \label{thm:all-real-pressure}
  423: Let \(\mathfrak P\) denote the Stern--Brocot pressure
  424: \[
  425:  \mathfrak P(\theta)
  426:  :=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac1d
  427:    \log\sum_{T\in\mathcal T_d}|T|^\theta .
  428: \]
  429: For every \(s\in\mathbb R\), the radius of convergence of \(B_s\) is
  430: \begin{equation}
  431:  R_B(s)=\exp\{-\mathfrak P(s/2)/2\}.
  432:  \label{eq:Bs-radius}
  433: \end{equation}
  434: Thus \(R_B(s)<1\) for \(s<2\), and \(R_B(s)=1\) for \(s\ge2\).
  435: The nonnegative real roots of \(B_s(r)=1\) are classified as follows:
  436: \[
  437: \begin{array}{c|c}
  438: s<\sigma_0 & \text{a unique root }r_s\in(0,R_B(s)),\\
  439: s=\sigma_0 & \text{the unique root }r_s=1=R_B(s),\\
  440: s>\sigma_0 & \text{no root in }[0,R_B(s)].
  441: \end{array}
  442: \]
  443: The pressure exists for every real tilt and satisfies
  444: \begin{equation}
  445:  P(-s):=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_{-s}(m)
  446:  =\begin{cases}
  447:    -\log r_s,&s<\sigma_0,\\
  448:    0,&s\ge\sigma_0.
  449:   \end{cases}
  450:  \label{eq:all-real-pressure}
  451: \end{equation}
  452: Equivalently, for \(t>-\sigma_0\), \(P(t)>0\) is the unique solution of
  453: \begin{equation}
  454:  \sum_{q=2}^{\infty}
  455:  \sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
  456:  q^t e^{-P(t)c(p/q)}=1,
  457:  \label{eq:exact-negative-pressure}
  458: \end{equation}
  459: whereas \(P(t)=0\) for \(t\le-\sigma_0\).  In particular,
  460: \begin{equation}
  461:  \{t\in\mathbb R:P(t)>0\}=(-\sigma_0,\infty).
  462:  \label{eq:exact-zero-pressure-set}
  463: \end{equation}
  464: 
  465: The restriction of \(P\) to \((-\sigma_0,\infty)\) is real analytic and
  466: strictly convex.  At \(t_c=-\sigma_0\), it has the finite one-sided
  467: derivatives
  468: \begin{equation}
  469:  P'_-(t_c)=0,
  470:  \qquad
  471:  P'_+(t_c)=
  472:  \frac{\displaystyle
  473:   \sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
  474:        q^{-\sigma_0}\log q}
  475:  {\displaystyle
  476:   \sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
  477:        c(p/q)q^{-\sigma_0}}>0.
  478:  \label{eq:critical-pressure-slopes}
  479: \end{equation}
  480: Consequently, \(P\) is not differentiable at the freezing point.
  481: \end{theorem}
  482: 
  483: \begin{proof}
  484: Write \(a_d(s):=b_{2d+1}(s)\).  The negative-continued-fraction
  485: parameterization gives
  486: \[
  487:  a_d(s)=\sum_{\substack{0<p<q,\ (p,q)=1\\d(p/q)=d}}q^{-s}.
  488: \]
  489: There are \(2^{d-1}\) fractions in this layer, and their denominators satisfy
  490: \(d+1\le q\le F_{d+2}\).  In particular, every \(B_s\) has positive finite
  491: radius.  The layer is precisely the new-denominator layer of the Knauf model.
  492: The published Stern--Brocot/Knauf pressure comparison
  493: \cite{FialaKlebanOzluk2003,KessebohmerStratmann2007} gives, for fixed \(s<2\),
  494: \begin{equation}
  495:  a_d(s)\asymp_s \exp\{d\mathfrak P(s/2)\},
  496:  \label{eq:knauf-layer-pressure}
  497: \end{equation}
  498: and gives
  499: \(d^{-1}\log a_d(s)\to0\) for \(s\ge2\).  Here only this published
  500: denominator-layer estimate is imported.  Cauchy--Hadamard now proves
  501: \eqref{eq:Bs-radius}.  Moreover, \eqref{eq:knauf-layer-pressure} shows that
  502: the terms of \(B_s\) do not tend to zero on \(|z|=R_B(s)\) when \(s<2\).
  503: At \(s=2\), the positive real boundary also diverges.  For \(s>2\), Tonelli's
  504: theorem gives
  505: \begin{equation}
  506:  B_s(1)=\sum_{q\ge2}\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(q)}{q^s}
  507:  =\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}-1<\infty.
  508:  \label{eq:weighted-renewal-at-one}
  509: \end{equation}
  510: 
  511: The coefficients are nonnegative and the support contains both \(3\) and
  512: \(5\), so \(B_s\) is strictly increasing on its positive interval of
  513: convergence.  If \(s\le2\), its positive boundary value is infinite; if
  514: \(2<s<\sigma_0\), \eqref{eq:weighted-renewal-at-one} is greater than one.
  515: These facts give the unique interior root for \(s<\sigma_0\).  At
  516: \(s=\sigma_0\), the boundary value is one, while it is less than one for
  517: \(s>\sigma_0\).  This proves the asserted real-root classification.  The
  518: triangle inequality also shows that, for \(s<\sigma_0\), the only zero of
  519: \(1-B_s(z)\) in \(|z|\le r_s\) is the simple zero \(r_s\): equality forces
  520: the degree-three and degree-five terms to have the same positive phase, and
  521: \(\gcd(3,5)=1\).
  522: 
  523: Concatenation of generating words gives
  524: \(u_{m+n}(s)\ge u_m(s)u_n(s)\).  The letters of costs \(3\) and \(5\) imply
  525: \(u_j(s)>0\) for every \(j\ge8\).  The shifted Fekete argument used in the
  526: proof of the strip result therefore applies without any restriction on
  527: \(s\), and yields
  528: \[
  529:  \gamma_s:=\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac1j\log u_j(s).
  530: \]
  531: If \(s<\sigma_0\), the formal identity
  532: \(U_s=(1-B_s)^{-1}\), positivity, and the preceding root classification show
  533: that the radius of \(U_s\) is \(r_s\).  At \(s=\sigma_0\), it is one.  If
  534: \(s>\sigma_0\), then \(U_s(1)=(1-B_s(1))^{-1}<\infty\), while
  535: \(u_j(s)\ge b_j(s)\) prevents the radius of \(U_s\) from exceeding
  536: \(R_B(s)=1\).  Hence
  537: \[
  538:  R_U(s)=\begin{cases}r_s,&s<\sigma_0,\\1,&s\ge\sigma_0,
  539:  \end{cases}
  540:  \qquad \gamma_s=-\log R_U(s)\ge0.
  541: \]
  542: For \(H_m(s)=\sum_{j=0}^m u_j(s)\), the exact renewal identity gives
  543: \[
  544:  H_m(s)\le S_{-s}(m)\le4H_{m+1}(s).
  545: \]
  546: Since \(m^{-1}\log H_m(s)\to\gamma_s\), this proves
  547: \eqref{eq:all-real-pressure} and the root equation.
  548: 
  549: For \(t>-\sigma_0\), put
  550: \[
  551:  \mu_t(p/q):=q^t e^{-P(t)c(p/q)}.
  552: \]
  553: The root equation makes this a probability measure.  The root lies strictly
  554: inside the relevant convergence circle, and
  555: \(\log q\ll d(p/q)\), so the defining series and all its parameter
  556: derivatives converge locally uniformly.  The analytic implicit-function
  557: theorem applies.  With \(X=\log q\) and \(C=c(p/q)\), differentiation gives
  558: \begin{equation}
  559:  P'(t)=\frac{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}X}{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}C},
  560:  \qquad
  561:  P''(t)=
  562:  \frac{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}[(X-P'(t)C)^2]}
  563:       {\mathbb E_{\mu_t}C}.
  564:  \label{eq:pressure-derivatives}
  565: \end{equation}
  566: The fractions \(1/2\) and \(1/3\) have \((C,q)=(3,2)\) and \((5,3)\),
  567: and \(\log2/3\ne\log3/5\).  Thus the variance in
  568: \eqref{eq:pressure-derivatives} is strictly positive.
  569: 
  570: It remains to justify passage to the critical endpoint.  For the canonical
  571: regular continued fraction \(p/q=[0;a_1,\ldots,a_r]\), with \(a_r>1\),
  572: the conversion between regular and negative continued fractions gives
  573: \[
  574:  d(p/q)=a_1+\cdots+a_r-1,
  575:  \qquad c(p/q)=2(a_1+\cdots+a_r)-1.
  576: \]
  577: The fixed-denominator mean theorem of Panov and Liehl
  578: \cite{Panov1982,Liehl1983,AistleitnerBordaHauke2023} states that
  579: \[
  580:  \frac1{\phi_{\rm E}(q)}
  581:  \sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}(a_1+\cdots+a_r)
  582:  \sim\frac6{\pi^2}(\log q)^2.
  583: \]
  584: Consequently, both the numerator and denominator in
  585: \eqref{eq:critical-pressure-slopes} converge absolutely, since they are
  586: bounded by constant multiples of
  587: \(\sum q^{1-\sigma_0}\log q\) and
  588: \(\sum q^{1-\sigma_0}(\log q)^2\), respectively.  Dominated convergence in
  589: the first identity of \eqref{eq:pressure-derivatives} gives the stated finite
  590: positive right derivative.  The pressure is identically zero to the left of
  591: \(t_c\), so its left derivative is zero.
  592: \end{proof}
  593: 
  594: \begin{theorem}[Full large deviations across the freezing corner]
  595: \label{thm:full-fiber-ldp}
  596: Let \(\mathbf X_m\) be uniformly distributed on \(X_m\), and set
  597: \[
  598:  \begin{gathered}
  599:  y_m(x):=\frac1m\log d_m(x),\qquad
  600:  Y_m:=y_m(\mathbf X_m),\\
  601:  t_c:=-\sigma_0,\qquad v_c:=P'_+(t_c),
  602:  \qquad \alpha_+:=\frac12\log\varphi.
  603:  \end{gathered}
  604: \]
  605: Then \((Y_m)\) satisfies a full large deviation principle with speed \(m\)
  606: and good rate function
  607: \begin{equation}
  608:  I(\alpha)=\sup_{t\in\mathbb R}
  609:  \{t\alpha-P(t)+\log\varphi\}.
  610:  \label{eq:full-fiber-ldp-rate}
  611: \end{equation}
  612: Its effective domain is \([0,\alpha_+]\).  On the coexistence interval,
  613: \begin{equation}
  614:  I(\alpha)=\log\varphi-\sigma_0\alpha
  615:  \qquad(0\le\alpha\le v_c).
  616:  \label{eq:frozen-affine-rate}
  617: \end{equation}
  618: More precisely, for every \(0<\alpha<v_c\),
  619: \begin{equation}
  620:  \begin{aligned}
  621:  &\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\liminf_{m\to\infty}
  622:  \frac1m\log\Pr\{|Y_m-\alpha|<\varepsilon\}\\
  623:  &\quad=
  624:  \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\limsup_{m\to\infty}
  625:  \frac1m\log\Pr\{|Y_m-\alpha|<\varepsilon\}\\
  626:  &\quad=-\bigl(\log\varphi-\sigma_0\alpha\bigr).
  627:  \end{aligned}
  628:  \label{eq:frozen-local-large-deviation}
  629: \end{equation}
  630: \end{theorem}
  631: 
  632: \begin{proof}
  633: For \(\alpha\in\mathbb R\) and \(\varepsilon>0\), write
  634: \[
  635:  A_m(\alpha,\varepsilon)
  636:  :=\card{\left\{x\in X_m:
  637:  \left|m^{-1}\log d_m(x)-\alpha\right|<\varepsilon\right\}}.
  638: \]
  639: We first establish the lower bound at the nonexposed slopes.  Fix
  640: \(t>t_c\), put \(v=P'(t)>v_c\), and define the tilted law on \(X_n\) by
  641: \[
  642:  \nu_{n,t}(x):=\frac{d_n(x)^t}{S_t(n)}.
  643: \]
  644: For sufficiently small \(s>0\), the exponential Markov inequality and
  645: \Cref{thm:all-real-pressure} give
  646: \[
  647:  \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac1n
  648:  \log\nu_{n,t}\{y_n\ge v+\delta\}
  649:  \le P(t+s)-P(t)-s(v+\delta)<0.
  650: \]
  651: The corresponding estimate with \(s<0\) gives the same conclusion for
  652: \(y_n\le v-\delta\).  Consequently,
  653: \[
  654:  \sum_{\substack{x\in X_n\\|y_n(x)-v|<\delta}}d_n(x)^t
  655:  =\exp\{n(P(t)+o(1))\}.
  656: \]
  657: Every summand on the left is at most
  658: \(\exp\{n(tv+|t|\delta)\}\), and hence
  659: \begin{equation}
  660:  \liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac1n\log A_n(v,\delta)
  661:  \ge P(t)-tv-|t|\delta.
  662:  \label{eq:exposed-source-count}
  663: \end{equation}
  664: 
  665: We now transfer these source states to a larger layer without changing their
  666: multiplicities.  The proof of \Cref{thm:fiber-partition-interval} gives the
  667: explicit bijection
  668: \[
  669:  \chi_n(x)=
  670:  \begin{cases}
  671:   F_{n+2}+V_n(x),&0\le V_n(x)\le F_{n+1}-2,\\
  672:   V_n(x),&F_{n+1}-1\le V_n(x)<F_{n+2},
  673:  \end{cases}
  674: \]
  675: from \(X_n\) onto the integer points of
  676: \(\mathcal I_n\sqcup\mathcal I_{n+1}\), with
  677: \(d_n(x)=R(\chi_n(x))\).  Take
  678: \(\delta<v/2\), so their multiplicities exceed two for all sufficiently
  679: large \(n\).  The uniform layer-count lemma shows that each Weinstein
  680: generating orbit supplies at most four source states to these two layers.
  681: The specialized formulas in the proof of
  682: \Cref{prop:weighted-generator-renewal} give
  683: \[
  684:  L([a]g)=L(g)+2a,\qquad
  685:  L([a]\tau g)=L(g)+1+2a,
  686: \]
  687: and the two sigma branches increase these lengths by one.  Hence every orbit
  688: meeting \(\mathcal I_n\sqcup\mathcal I_{n+1}\) has generator length at most
  689: \(n+1\).
  690: Keeping the generating number of every orbit which occurs therefore gives a
  691: set \(\mathcal G_n\) such that
  692: \begin{equation}
  693:  \card{\mathcal G_n}\ge\frac14 A_n(v,\delta),
  694:  \qquad L(g)\le n+1\quad(g\in\mathcal G_n),
  695:  \label{eq:source-generating-orbits}
  696: \end{equation}
  697: and \(n^{-1}\log R(g)\in(v-\delta,v+\delta)\), since the orbit action
  698: preserves \(R\).  Distinct elements of
  699: \(\mathcal G_n\) generate disjoint orbits.
  700: 
  701: Fix \(0<\alpha<v_c\), set \(\rho=\alpha/v\), and let
  702: \(n_m=\lfloor\rho m\rfloor\).  For sufficiently large \(m\), every
  703: \(g\in\mathcal G_{n_m}\) has \(L(g)<m\).  The generator formulas in the
  704: proof of \Cref{prop:weighted-generator-renewal} permit the parity-free
  705: definition
  706: \[
  707:  z_m(g):=
  708:  \begin{cases}
  709:  [a]g,&m-L(g)=2a,\\
  710:  [a]\tau g,&m-L(g)=2a+1.
  711:  \end{cases}
  712: \]
  713: They give \(L(z_m(g))=m\) and \(R(z_m(g))=R(g)>1\).  Thus
  714: \(z_m(g)\in\mathcal I_m\), and \(\chi_m^{-1}\) turns these into distinct
  715: states of \(X_m\).  Moreover,
  716: \[
  717:  \left|m^{-1}\log R(z_m(g))-\alpha\right|
  718:  \le \frac{n_m}{m}\delta+\frac vm.
  719: \]
  720: Combining this injection with
  721: \eqref{eq:exposed-source-count}--\eqref{eq:source-generating-orbits} yields,
  722: for every \(\varepsilon>\rho\delta\),
  723: \begin{equation}
  724:  \liminf_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log A_m(\alpha,\varepsilon)
  725:  \ge \rho\bigl(P(t)-tv-|t|\delta\bigr).
  726:  \label{eq:orbit-filling-lower-bound}
  727: \end{equation}
  728: First let \(\delta\downarrow0\), and then let \(t\downarrow t_c\).  Since
  729: \(P(t)\to0\), \(P'(t)=v\to v_c\), and
  730: \[
  731:  \rho(P(t)-tv)=\alpha\left(\frac{P(t)}v-t\right)
  732:  \longrightarrow-t_c\alpha=\sigma_0\alpha,
  733: \]
  734: we obtain
  735: \begin{equation}
  736:  \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\liminf_{m\to\infty}
  737:  \frac1m\log A_m(\alpha,\varepsilon)
  738:  \ge\sigma_0\alpha.
  739:  \label{eq:frozen-count-lower}
  740: \end{equation}
  741: 
  742: The matching upper bound uses the critical pressure directly.  On the set
  743: counted by \(A_m(\alpha,\varepsilon)\),
  744: \(d_m(x)^{-\sigma_0}\ge
  745: \exp\{-\sigma_0m(\alpha+\varepsilon)\}\).  Therefore
  746: \[
  747:  A_m(\alpha,\varepsilon)
  748:  e^{-\sigma_0m(\alpha+\varepsilon)}
  749:  \le S_{-\sigma_0}(m)=e^{o(m)},
  750: \]
  751: which, together with \eqref{eq:frozen-count-lower}, proves the corresponding
  752: counting limit.  Since \(\card{X_m}=F_{m+2}\) and
  753: \(m^{-1}\log F_{m+2}\to\log\varphi\), this is
  754: \eqref{eq:frozen-local-large-deviation}.
  755: 
  756: We next identify the convex dual.  If \(0\le\alpha\le v_c\), then
  757: \(P(t)=0\) for \(t\le t_c\), while convexity and the right derivative at
  758: \(t_c\) give \(P(t)\ge v_c(t-t_c)\) for \(t\ge t_c\).  It follows that
  759: \(t\alpha-P(t)\le t_c\alpha\) for every \(t\), with equality at
  760: \(t=t_c\), proving \eqref{eq:frozen-affine-rate}.
  761: 
  762: The exact maximum theorem gives
  763: \[
  764:  \max_{x\in X_m}m^{-1}\log d_m(x)\longrightarrow\alpha_+.
  765: \]
  766: For \(t>0\), the bounds
  767: \(D_m^t\le S_t(m)\le F_{m+2}D_m^t\) imply
  768: \[
  769:  \alpha_+\le\frac{P(t)}t
  770:  \le\alpha_++\frac{\log\varphi}{t}.
  771: \]
  772: By convexity, (P') is increasing and therefore has a limit in
  773: \((0,+\infty]\).  The identity
  774: \[
  775:  \frac{P(t)}t=\frac{P(0)}t+\frac1t\int_0^t P'(u)\,du
  776: \]
  777: and Ces\`aro convergence show that this limit is \(\alpha_+\).  Thus
  778: \(P'(t)\uparrow\alpha_+\) as \(t\to\infty\).  Strict convexity and
  779: analyticity on \((t_c,\infty)\) now show that every
  780: \(\alpha\in(v_c,\alpha_+)\) is exposed by a unique \(t>t_c\).  The tilted
  781: estimate above with \(n=m\) supplies
  782: \begin{equation}
  783:  \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\liminf_{m\to\infty}
  784:  \frac1m\log\Pr\{|Y_m-\alpha|<\varepsilon\}
  785:  \ge P(t)-t\alpha-\log\varphi=-I(\alpha).
  786:  \label{eq:exposed-local-large-deviation}
  787: \end{equation}
  788: 
  789: Finally, the logarithmic moment generating functions under the uniform law
  790: converge on all of \(\mathbb R\) to
  791: \[
  792:  \Lambda(t)=P(t)-\log\varphi.
  793: \]
  794: The variables are eventually supported in a fixed compact interval, so they
  795: are exponentially tight.  The G\"artner--Ellis upper bound
  796: \cite[Theorem~2.3.6(a)]{DemboZeitouni2010} therefore gives the
  797: large-deviation upper bound for all closed sets.
  798: 
  799: It remains to justify the effective domain and endpoint lower bounds.  If
  800: \(\alpha<0\), letting \(t\to-\infty\) in
  801: \eqref{eq:full-fiber-ldp-rate} and using \(P(t)=0\) gives
  802: \(I(\alpha)=+\infty\).  If \(\alpha>\alpha_+\), the limit
  803: \(P(t)/t\to\alpha_+\) gives the same conclusion as \(t\to\infty\).
  804: Conversely, if \(0\le\alpha\le\alpha_+\), then
  805: \(t\alpha-P(t)\) is bounded above on \(( -\infty,t_c]\), on the compact
  806: interval \([t_c,0]\), and on \([0,\infty)\), where
  807: \(P(t)\ge t\alpha_+\).  Thus the effective domain is exactly
  808: \([0,\alpha_+]\).  The rate is a lower-semicontinuous convex function, and
  809: its finiteness on this closed interval implies continuity relative to the
  810: interval, including at the endpoints.
  811: 
  812: The local lower bounds already proved cover the coexistence and exposed
  813: parts of \((0,\alpha_+)\), and points from either side approximate \(v_c\).
  814: At \(0\), the two singleton fibers give the lower bound directly.  At
  815: \(\alpha_+\), continuity of \(I\) and exposed points approaching from below
  816: supply the lower bound for every open neighborhood.  Hence the lower bound
  817: holds for every open set.  The effective domain is compact and \(I\) is
  818: lower semicontinuous, so \(I\) is good and the full large deviation principle
  819: follows.
  820: \end{proof}
  821: 
  822: \subsection{Microcanonical phase separation}
  823: 
  824: The renewal description also retains the generating layer, rather than only
  825: the multiplicity.  Let
  826: \[
  827:  \mathcal A:=\{p/q:0<p<q,\ (p,q)=1\},
  828:  \qquad \pi(p/q):=q^{-\sigma _0}.
  829: \]
  830: The critical identity \eqref{eq:weighted-renewal-at-one} gives
  831: \(\sum_{\mathcal A}\pi=1\).  Let \((C_i,H_i)_{i\ge1}\) be independent with
  832: \[
  833:  \Pr\{(C_i,H_i)=(c(p/q),\log q)\}=\pi(p/q),
  834: \]
  835: put \(S_n=\sum_{i=1}^nC_i\), \(T_n=\sum_{i=1}^nH_i\), and define the marked
  836: renewal measure
  837: \[
  838:  \mathcal U:=\sum_{n\ge1}\mathcal L(S_n,T_n).
  839: \]
  840: The moment estimates in the proof of \Cref{thm:all-real-pressure} show that
  841: \[
  842:  \mu_C:=\mathbb E C_1<\infty,
  843:  \qquad \mu_H:=\mathbb E H_1<\infty,
  844:  \qquad \frac{\mu_H}{\mu_C}=v_c.
  845: \]
  846: 
  847: We shall use the following marked form of the already proved weighted renewal
  848: identity.  It is recorded explicitly to retain the generating cost in the
  849: conditional argument.  For $x\in X_m$ with \(d_m(x)>2\), let \(g_m(x)\) be
  850: the unique generating number of the Weinstein orbit containing \(\chi_m(x)\).
  851: Define
  852: \[
  853:  K_m(c):=
  854:  \begin{cases}
  855:   4,&c\le m-1,\\
  856:   3,&c=m,\\
  857:   1,&c=m+1,\\
  858:   0,&c\ge m+2.
  859:  \end{cases}
  860: \]
  861: For every Borel set \(B\subset\mathbb R\times(\log2,\infty)\),
  862: \begin{equation}
  863:  \card{\{x\in X_m:(L(g_m(x)),\log d_m(x))\in B\}}
  864:  =\int_B K_m(s)e^{\sigma_0t}\,\mathcal U(ds,dt).
  865:  \label{eq:marked-microcanonical-count}
  866: \end{equation}
  867: Indeed, for a generating number \(g\) of cost \(c=L(g)\), the four branches
  868: and their layer indices are
  869: \[
  870:  \begin{array}{c|c}
  871:  {}[a]g&c+2a\\
  872:  {}[a]\tau g&c+1+2a\\
  873:  \sigma([a]g)&c+1+2a\\
  874:  \sigma([a]\tau g)&c+2+2a.
  875:  \end{array}
  876: \]
  877: The free action makes the middle two points distinct.  Thus this orbit
  878: contributes zero, one, or two points to \(\mathcal I_j\), according as
  879: \(j<c\), \(j=c\), or \(j>c\).  Summing over the two disjoint layers
  880: \(\mathcal I_m\sqcup\mathcal I_{m+1}\) gives exactly \(K_m(c)\).  The two
  881: unit-multiplicity endpoints correspond to the empty word and are excluded by
  882: the condition \(t>\log2\).
  883: 
  884: Under Weinstein's free-monoid bijection, a nonempty word
  885: \(w=(p_1/q_1)\times\cdots\times(p_n/q_n)\) has
  886: \[
  887:  L(g)=S_n,\qquad \log R(g)=T_n,
  888:  \qquad \pi(w)=e^{-\sigma_0T_n}.
  889: \]
  890: Multiplication of its renewal mass by \(e^{\sigma_0T_n}\), followed by the
  891: orbit count \(K_m(S_n)\), therefore counts every corresponding finite-window
  892: state once.  This proves \eqref{eq:marked-microcanonical-count}.
  893: 
  894: \begin{theorem}[Finite-window conditional phase separation]

exec

 succeeded in 186ms:
  894: \begin{theorem}[Finite-window conditional phase separation]
  895: \label{thm:conditional-phase-separation}
  896: Fix \(0<\alpha<v_c\) and
  897: \[
  898:  0<\varepsilon<\min\{\alpha,v_c-\alpha\}.
  899: \]
  900: Let \(\mathbf X_{m,\alpha,\varepsilon}\) be uniform on
  901: \[
  902:  \left\{x\in X_m:
  903:  \left|m^{-1}\log d_m(x)-\alpha\right|<\varepsilon\right\}.
  904: \]
  905: This set is nonempty for all sufficiently large \(m\), and
  906: \begin{equation}
  907:  \left(
  908:   \frac1m\log d_m(\mathbf X_{m,\alpha,\varepsilon}),
  909:   \frac1mL(g_m(\mathbf X_{m,\alpha,\varepsilon}))
  910:  \right)
  911:  \xrightarrow[m\to\infty]{\Pr}
  912:  \left(\alpha+\varepsilon,
  913:        \frac{\alpha+\varepsilon}{v_c}\right).
  914:  \label{eq:conditional-phase-separation}
  915: \end{equation}
  916: Consequently, for every \(\eta>0\),
  917: \[
  918:  \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\lim_{m\to\infty}
  919:  \Pr\left\{
  920:   \left|m^{-1}L(g_m(\mathbf X_{m,\alpha,\varepsilon}))
  921:        -\frac\alpha{v_c}\right|>\eta
  922:  \right\}=0.
  923: \]
  924: For fixed \(\varepsilon\), the outer limit is essential: if
  925: \(0<\eta<\varepsilon/v_c\), then the probability in the last display tends
  926: to one.
  927: \end{theorem}
  928: 
  929: \begin{proof}
  930: Put \(a=\alpha-\varepsilon\), \(b=\alpha+\varepsilon\), and
  931: \(r=\mu_C/\mu_H=1/v_c\).  We first record the two renewal facts used below.
  932: For each fixed \(h>0\), Blackwell's nonarithmetic renewal theorem
  933: \cite[Ch.~XI]{Feller1971} gives
  934: \begin{equation}
  935:  \mathcal U\{t<T\le t+h\}\longrightarrow\frac h{\mu_H}.
  936:  \label{eq:blackwell-critical-renewal}
  937: \end{equation}
  938: The law of \(H_1\) is nonarithmetic because it charges both \(\log2\) and
  939: \(\log3\), whose ratio is irrational.  The strong law gives
  940: \(S_n/T_n\to r\) almost surely.  Moreover, since \(H_i\ge\log2\), an interval
  941: of length \(h\) contains at most
  942: \(N_h:=\lfloor h/\log2\rfloor+1\) renewal epochs on every sample path.
  943: Dominated convergence therefore yields, for each \(\delta>0\),
  944: \begin{equation}
  945:  \mathcal U\{t<T\le t+h,\ |S/T-r|>\delta\}\longrightarrow0.
  946:  \label{eq:marked-renewal-reward-law}
  947: \end{equation}
  948: 
  949: Choose \(\delta_0>0\) so that \((r+\delta_0)b<1\), and fix \(h>0\).
  950: On
  951: \[
  952:  J_m=(bm-2h,bm-h]
  953: \]
  954: the part of \(\mathcal U\) satisfying \(|S/T-r|\le\delta_0\) has mass
  955: \(h/\mu_H+o(1)\) by
  956: \eqref{eq:blackwell-critical-renewal}--\eqref{eq:marked-renewal-reward-law}.
  957: For all sufficiently large \(m\), every such point has \(S\le m-1\), and
  958: hence \(K_m(S)=4\).  If \(Z_m\) denotes the cardinality of the conditioning
  959: set, \eqref{eq:marked-microcanonical-count} gives
  960: \begin{equation}
  961:  Z_m\ge4e^{\sigma_0(bm-2h)}
  962:        \left(\frac h{\mu_H}+o(1)\right).
  963:  \label{eq:conditional-denominator-lower}
  964: \end{equation}
  965: 
  966: For fixed \(\delta>0\), set
  967: \[
  968:  \rho_m:=\sup_{t\ge am-h}
  969:  \mathcal U\{t<T\le t+h,\ |S/T-r|>\delta\}.
  970: \]
  971: Equation \eqref{eq:marked-renewal-reward-law} implies \(\rho_m\to0\).
  972: Covering \((am,bm)\) from its upper endpoint by intervals of length \(h\),
  973: and using \(K_m\le4\), bounds the number of exceptional states by
  974: \[
  975:  \frac{4e^{\sigma_0bm}}{1-e^{-\sigma_0h}}\rho_m.
  976: \]
  977: After division by \eqref{eq:conditional-denominator-lower}, this proves
  978: \[
  979:  \frac{L(g_m)}{\log d_m}=\frac ST\xrightarrow{\Pr}r
  980: \]
  981: under the conditional law.
  982: 
  983: Finally, \(\mathcal U\{t<T\le t+h\}\le N_h\) for every \(t\).  Thus, for
  984: each \(\gamma>0\), the number of conditioned states with
  985: \(T\le(b-\gamma)m\) is
  986: \(O(me^{\sigma_0(b-\gamma)m})\).  Equation
  987: \eqref{eq:conditional-denominator-lower} shows that their conditional
  988: probability tends to zero.  Since the conditioning itself gives
  989: \(T/m<b\), we have \(T/m\to b\) in probability.  Combining this with
  990: \(S/T\to r\) proves \eqref{eq:conditional-phase-separation}; its stated
  991: consequences follow immediately.
  992: \end{proof}
  993: 
  994: \begin{corollary}[Admissible shrinking windows]
  995: \label{cor:shrinking-window-phase-separation}
  996: Under the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:conditional-phase-separation}, suppose that
  997: \(\varepsilon_m\downarrow0\) and
  998: \(\liminf_{m\to\infty}m\varepsilon_m>0\).  Then the conditioning sets are
  999: nonempty for all sufficiently large \(m\), and
 1000: \[
 1001:  \frac1mL(g_m(\mathbf X_{m,\alpha,\varepsilon_m}))
 1002:  \xrightarrow[m\to\infty]{\Pr}\frac\alpha{v_c}.
 1003: \]
 1004: \end{corollary}
 1005: 
 1006: \begin{proof}
 1007: Choose a fixed \(h>0\) smaller than
 1008: \(\liminf m\varepsilon_m\).  The interval
 1009: \(((\alpha+\varepsilon_m)m-2h,
 1010:    (\alpha+\varepsilon_m)m-h]\) then lies inside the conditioning window for
 1011: all sufficiently large \(m\).  The proof of
 1012: \Cref{thm:conditional-phase-separation}, with this fixed interval, gives the
 1013: same denominator lower bound and the same uniform estimate
 1014: \(S/T\to1/v_c\).  The conditioning itself now gives \(T/m\to\alpha\), which
 1015: proves the assertion.
 1016: \end{proof}
 1017: 
 1018: \begin{remark}[Arithmetic obstruction for thinner windows]
 1019: \label{rem:shrinking-window-arithmetic-obstruction}
 1020: The preceding corollary cannot be extended to every sequence
 1021: \(\varepsilon_m\downarrow0\).  Put
 1022: \(\mathcal D_m=\{\log d_m(x):x\in X_m\}\).  For every
 1023: \(0<\alpha<v_c\), there are infinitely many \(m\) for which
 1024: \(\alpha m\notin\mathcal D_m\).  Indeed, otherwise \(e^{\alpha m}\) and
 1025: \(e^{\alpha(m+1)}\) would be integers for all large \(m\), forcing
 1026: \(e^\alpha\) to be an integer; this contradicts
 1027: \(0<\alpha<v_c\le\alpha_+=\tfrac12\log\varphi<\log2\).
 1028: Enumerate such a subsequence as \((m_j)\).  Starting from any
 1029: \(\varepsilon_1>0\), define the remaining widths recursively so that
 1030: \(0<\varepsilon_m<\varepsilon_{m-1}/2\), and, when \(m=m_j\), impose also
 1031: \[
 1032:  \varepsilon_m<\frac1m
 1033:  \operatorname{dist}(\alpha m,\mathcal D_m).
 1034: \]
 1035: This gives a decreasing sequence for which the conditioning set is empty at
 1036: every \(m_j\).  Thus windows on a smaller scale require an additional
 1037: arithmetic local theorem, not merely a uniform version of Blackwell renewal.
 1038: \end{remark}
 1039: 
 1040: The preceding obstruction concerns arbitrary thin windows.  A complete
 1041: arithmetic conditional limit can nevertheless be obtained along the dyadic
 1042: multiplier ray.  For \(r\ge1\), let
 1043: \[
 1044:  \mathcal G_r:=\{g:\text{\(g\) is a Weinstein generator and }R(g)=2^r\}.
 1045: \]
 1046: 
 1047: \begin{theorem}[Exact dyadic multiplier limit]
 1048: \label{thm:dyadic-exact-multiplier}
 1049: The series
 1050: \begin{equation}
 1051:  \gamma_2:=\frac13\sum_{a\ge1}3^{-a}
 1052:  \sum_{\substack{1\le p<2^a\\p\ \mathrm{odd}}}c(p/2^a)
 1053:  \label{eq:dyadic-cost-velocity}
 1054: \end{equation}
 1055: converges.  Moreover,
 1056: \[
 1057:  \card{\mathcal G_r}=3^{r-1}\qquad(r\ge1).
 1058: \]
 1059: If \(G_r\) is uniform on \(\mathcal G_r\), then
 1060: \begin{equation}
 1061:  \frac{L(G_r)}r\xrightarrow[r\to\infty]{\Pr}\gamma_2.
 1062:  \label{eq:dyadic-generator-bridge}
 1063: \end{equation}
 1064: 
 1065: Let \(r_m\to\infty\) be integers such that
 1066: \[
 1067:  \frac m{r_m}\longrightarrow\beta>\gamma_2.
 1068: \]
 1069: Then
 1070: \begin{equation}
 1071:  C_m(2^{r_m})\sim4\cdot3^{r_m-1}.
 1072:  \label{eq:dyadic-finite-window-count}
 1073: \end{equation}
 1074: In particular, the exact level \(\{x\in X_m:d_m(x)=2^{r_m}\}\) is nonempty
 1075: for all sufficiently large \(m\).  If \(\mathbf X_m^{(2)}\) is uniform on
 1076: this level, then
 1077: \begin{equation}
 1078:  \frac{L(g_m(\mathbf X_m^{(2)}))}{r_m}
 1079:  \xrightarrow[m\to\infty]{\Pr}\gamma_2,
 1080:  \qquad
 1081:  \frac{L(g_m(\mathbf X_m^{(2)}))}{m}
 1082:  \xrightarrow[m\to\infty]{\Pr}\frac{\gamma_2}{\beta}.
 1083:  \label{eq:dyadic-finite-window-bridge}
 1084: \end{equation}
 1085: Equivalently, with \(v_2=\log2/\gamma_2\), if
 1086: \(r_m=\lfloor\alpha m/\log2\rfloor\) and \(0<\alpha<v_2\), the last limit is
 1087: \(\alpha/v_2\).
 1088: \end{theorem}
 1089: 
 1090: \begin{proof}
 1091: If a free generator word has multiplier \(2^r\), every denominator in the
 1092: word is a power of \(2\).  For each \(a\ge1\), there are
 1093: \(\phi_{\rm E}(2^a)=2^{a-1}\) letters with denominator \(2^a\).  Hence the
 1094: ordinary generating function for such words, including the empty word, is
 1095: \[
 1096:  \frac1{1-\sum_{a\ge1}2^{a-1}z^a}
 1097:  =\frac{1-2z}{1-3z}.
 1098: \]
 1099: Coefficient extraction proves \(\card{\mathcal G_r}=3^{r-1}\) for \(r\ge1\).
 1100: 
 1101: Define an independent letter sequence \((A_i,C_i)_{i\ge1}\) by
 1102: \[
 1103:  \Pr\{(A_i,C_i)=(a,c(p/2^a))\}=3^{-a}
 1104:  \quad(a\ge1,\ 1\le p<2^a,\ p\ \mathrm{odd}).
 1105: \]
 1106: This is a probability distribution because
 1107: \[
 1108:  \sum_{a\ge1}2^{a-1}3^{-a}=1,
 1109:  \qquad \mathbb E A_1=3.
 1110: \]
 1111: For the canonical regular continued fraction of \(p/2^a\), the
 1112: Panov--Liehl fixed-denominator mean theorem
 1113: \cite{Panov1982,Liehl1983,AistleitnerBordaHauke2023} gives, as \(a\to\infty\),
 1114: \[
 1115:  2^{1-a}\sum_{\substack{1\le p<2^a\\p\ \mathrm{odd}}}
 1116:  c(p/2^a)=O(a^2).
 1117: \]
 1118: Consequently \(\mathbb E C_1<\infty\), and
 1119: \(\mathbb E C_1/\mathbb E A_1=\gamma_2\).
 1120: 
 1121: Put \(A^{(n)}=\sum_{i=1}^nA_i\) and
 1122: \(C^{(n)}=\sum_{i=1}^nC_i\).  Every word with total exponent \(r\) has
 1123: probability \(3^{-r}\).  Therefore
 1124: \[
 1125:  \Pr\{A^{(n)}=r\text{ for some }n\}
 1126:  =3^{-r}\card{\mathcal G_r}=\frac13.
 1127: \]
 1128: Conditioned on this renewal event, the word is uniform on \(\mathcal G_r\).
 1129: The strong law gives
 1130: \(C^{(n)}/A^{(n)}\to\mathbb E C_1/\mathbb E A_1=\gamma_2\) almost surely.
 1131: On every sample path, the indicator that a renewal visit to \(r\) violates a
 1132: fixed neighborhood of this limit therefore tends to zero as \(r\to\infty\).
 1133: Dominated convergence, followed by division by the constant hitting
 1134: probability \(1/3\), proves \eqref{eq:dyadic-generator-bridge}.
 1135: 
 1136: For \(j\ge0\), let
 1137: \[
 1138:  U_j(2^r):=\card{\{g\in\mathcal G_r:L(g)=j\}}.
 1139: \]
 1140: The four-branch formula \eqref{eq:marked-microcanonical-count} gives, for
 1141: \(r\ge2\),
 1142: \begin{equation}
 1143:  C_m(2^r)=\sum_{j\ge0}K_m(j)U_j(2^r).
 1144:  \label{eq:dyadic-four-branch-count}
 1145: \end{equation}
 1146: Under the hypotheses on \(r_m\),
 1147: \eqref{eq:dyadic-generator-bridge} implies
 1148: \[
 1149:  \Pr\{L(G_{r_m})\le m-1\}\longrightarrow1,
 1150: \]
 1151: because \(m/r_m\to\beta>\gamma_2\).  Since \(0\le K_m\le4\) and
 1152: \(K_m(j)=4\) for \(j\le m-1\), division of
 1153: \eqref{eq:dyadic-four-branch-count} by \(4\card{\mathcal G_{r_m}}\) proves
 1154: \eqref{eq:dyadic-finite-window-count}.  The cost law under the uniform exact
 1155: fiber level is obtained from the uniform law on \(\mathcal G_{r_m}\) by the
 1156: bounded weight \(K_m/4\), which converges to \(1\) in probability and in
 1157: \(L^1\).  Thus the two laws have total-variation distance tending to zero.
 1158: Equation \eqref{eq:dyadic-generator-bridge} now gives
 1159: \eqref{eq:dyadic-finite-window-bridge}.  The final formulation follows from
 1160: \(r_m/m\to\alpha/\log2\).
 1161: \end{proof}
 1162: 
 1163: \begin{remark}
 1164: The theorem identifies the quenched speed on one arithmetic multiplier ray.
 1165: The dyadic exponent--cost lattice has no proper sublattice obstruction:
 1166: the two letter vectors \((1,3)\) and \((2,7)\) have determinant \(1\).
 1167: The comparison with the critical annealed velocity requires the explicit
 1168: certificate given next.
 1169: \end{remark}
 1170: 
 1171: \begin{theorem}[Strict dyadic--critical speed separation]
 1172: \label{thm:strict-speed-separation}
 1173: The dyadic velocity in \Cref{thm:dyadic-exact-multiplier} and the critical
 1174: velocity in \Cref{thm:full-fiber-ldp} satisfy
 1175: \begin{equation}
 1176:  \begin{gathered}
 1177:   5.185556312796440793\le\gamma_2
 1178:   \le5.235362589164389880,\\
 1179:   v_2\ge0.132397168057576261,
 1180:  \end{gathered}
 1181:  \label{eq:dyadic-speed-certificate}
 1182: \end{equation}
 1183: and
 1184: \begin{equation}
 1185:  v_c\le0.131225207544812100.
 1186:  \label{eq:critical-speed-certificate}
 1187: \end{equation}
 1188: Consequently,
 1189: \begin{equation}
 1190:  v_2-v_c\ge0.001171960512764161>0.
 1191:  \label{eq:strict-speed-separation}
 1192: \end{equation}
 1193: 
 1194: In particular, fix \(0<\alpha<v_c\), put
 1195: \(r_m=\lfloor\alpha m/\log2\rfloor\), and condition uniformly on the exact
 1196: level \(d_m=2^{r_m}\).  This level is nonempty for all sufficiently large
 1197: \(m\), and
 1198: \begin{equation}
 1199:  \frac{L(g_m)}m\xrightarrow[m\to\infty]{\Pr}
 1200:  \frac{\alpha}{v_2}\ne\frac{\alpha}{v_c}.
 1201:  \label{eq:dyadic-uniform-speed-counterexample}
 1202: \end{equation}
 1203: Thus the critical conditional speed does not extend uniformly from bands to
 1204: all realizable exact multiplier sequences.
 1205: \end{theorem}
 1206: 
 1207: \begin{proof}
 1208: For a reduced fraction \(p/q\in(0,1)\), let \(S(p/q)\) denote the sum of the
 1209: partial quotients in its canonical regular continued fraction, so that
 1210: \(c(p/q)=2S(p/q)-1\).  Set
 1211: \[
 1212:  \begin{gathered}
 1213:   T(q):=\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}S(p/q),
 1214:   \qquad
 1215:   \mathcal C(q):=2T(q)-\phi_{\rm E}(q),\\
 1216:   A(q):=\sum_{n=1}^{q-1}\tau(n)\tau(q-n).
 1217:  \end{gathered}
 1218: \]
 1219: We first establish an explicit bound for \(\mathcal C(q)\).  Each of the
 1220: \(S(p/q)-1\) strict ancestor intervals of \(p/q\) in the Stern--Brocot tree
 1221: has ordered adjacent endpoints, say \(a/b<c/d\), and there are unique
 1222: positive integers \(u,v\) such that
 1223: \[
 1224:  \frac pq=\frac{ua+vc}{ub+vd}.
 1225: \]
 1226: Ordered coprime denominators \(b,d\) determine their Farey adjacent interval
 1227: uniquely.  Hence the map from a fraction together with one of its strict
 1228: ancestors to \((b,u,d,v)\) is injective, and
 1229: \begin{equation}
 1230:  T(q)-\phi_{\rm E}(q)\le A(q).
 1231:  \label{eq:ancestor-divisor-injection}
 1232: \end{equation}
 1233: Indeed, the quadruples on the right are counted by factoring
 1234: \(n=bu\) and \(q-n=dv\).
 1235: 
 1236: Use
 1237: \(\tau(n)\le2\sum_{e\mid n,\,e\le\sqrt n}1\), and let
 1238: \[
 1239:  N_q(e,f):=\card{\{1\le n<q:e\mid n,\ f\mid q-n\}}.
 1240: \]
 1241: If \(N_q(e,f)>0\), then \((e,f)\mid q\), and the Chinese remainder theorem
 1242: gives
 1243: \[
 1244:  N_q(e,f)\le\frac{q(e,f)}{ef}+1.
 1245: \]
 1246: Writing \(e=gh\), \(f=gk\), dropping the coprimality of \(h,k\), and using
 1247: the harmonic-sum bound gives
 1248: \[
 1249:  \sum_{\substack{e,f\le\sqrt q\\(e,f)\mid q}}
 1250:  \frac{(e,f)}{ef}
 1251:  \le\sum_{g\mid q}\frac1g
 1252:        \left(1+\frac12\log q\right)^2.
 1253: \]
 1254: For \(q=2^a\), the divisor sum is less than \(2\).  Since there are at most
 1255: \(q\) pairs \((e,f)\), it follows that
 1256: \[
 1257:  A(2^a)\le8\,2^a\left(1+\frac a2\log2\right)^2+4\,2^a.
 1258: \]
 1259: Combining this with \eqref{eq:ancestor-divisor-injection} and
 1260: \(\phi_{\rm E}(2^a)=2^{a-1}\) yields the all-range estimate
 1261: \begin{equation}
 1262:  \mathcal C(2^a)
 1263:  \le2^a\left\{\frac{17}{2}
 1264:        +16\left(1+\frac a2\log2\right)^2\right\}
 1265:  \qquad(a\ge1).
 1266:  \label{eq:dyadic-cost-sum-bound}
 1267: \end{equation}
 1268: The same argument for general \(q\), now using
 1269: \(\sum_{g\mid q}g^{-1}\le1+\log q\), also gives
 1270: \begin{equation}
 1271:  \mathcal C(q)\le q\{9+8(1+\log q)^3\}.
 1272:  \label{eq:general-cost-sum-bound}
 1273: \end{equation}
 1274: 
 1275: Exact Euclidean-algorithm enumeration through \(a=25\) gives
 1276: \[
 1277:  \gamma_2^{(25)}
 1278:  =\frac13\sum_{a=1}^{25}3^{-a}\mathcal C(2^a)
 1279:  =\frac{13180988392373}{2541865828329}.
 1280: \]
 1281: Using \(\log2<7/10\) in \eqref{eq:dyadic-cost-sum-bound}, the remaining
 1282: geometric quadratic sum is rational and satisfies
 1283: \[
 1284:  \begin{split}
 1285:  0\le\gamma_2-\gamma_2^{(25)}
 1286:  &\le\frac13\sum_{a=26}^{\infty}
 1287:   \left(\frac23\right)^a
 1288:   \left\{\frac{17}{2}+16\left(1+\frac{7a}{20}\right)^2\right\}\\
 1289:  &=\frac{126600871936}{2541865828329}.
 1290:  \end{split}
 1291: \]
 1292: Thus
 1293: \[
 1294:  \gamma_2\le\frac{4435863088103}{847288609443},
 1295: \]
 1296: which, with outward rounding of \(\log2\), proves
 1297: \eqref{eq:dyadic-speed-certificate}.
 1298: 
 1299: It remains to certify \(v_c\).  Put
 1300: \[
 1301:  R(s):=\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)},
 1302:  \qquad
 1303:  H:=-R'(\sigma_0),
 1304:  \qquad
 1305:  D:=\sum_{q\ge2}\mathcal C(q)q^{-\sigma_0}.
 1306: \]
 1307: Then \eqref{eq:critical-pressure-slopes} gives \(v_c=H/D\).  For completeness,
 1308: the interval computation uses, for \(s>1\),
 1309: \[
 1310:  \begin{split}
 1311:  Z_8(s)={}&\sum_{n=1}^{31}n^{-s}
 1312:  +\frac{32^{1-s}}{s-1}+\frac12 32^{-s}\\
 1313:  &+\sum_{k=1}^{8}\frac{B_{2k}}{(2k)!}
 1314:        (s)_{2k-1}32^{1-s-2k}.
 1315:  \end{split}
 1316: \]
 1317: The periodic-Bernoulli remainder and its differentiated form give
 1318: \begin{align*}
 1319:  |\zeta(s)-Z_8(s)|
 1320:  &\le\frac{|B_{16}|}{16!}(s)_{15}32^{-s-15},\\
 1321:  |\zeta'(s)-Z_8'(s)|
 1322:  &\le\frac{|B_{16}|}{16!}32^{-s-15}\\
 1323:  &\quad{}\times\left\{
 1324:   \frac{(s)_{16}'}{s+15}
 1325:   +(s)_{16}\left(\frac{\log32}{s+15}
 1326:                   +\frac1{(s+15)^2}\right)
 1327:  \right\}.
 1328: \end{align*}
 1329: Directed interval evaluation gives
 1330: \[
 1331:  \begin{split}
 1332:  R(2.4787)-2&\in
 1333:  [0.000131507893202250,0.000131507893202251],\\
 1334:  R(2.4788)-2&\in
 1335:  [-0.000127411416688770,-0.000127411416688769].
 1336:  \end{split}
 1337: \]
 1338: Since \(R\) is strictly decreasing,
 1339: \(\sigma_0\in[2.4787,2.4788]\).  Moreover,
 1340: \(-R'(s)=\sum_{q\ge2}\phi_{\rm E}(q)(\log q)q^{-s}\) is strictly decreasing.
 1341: The same interval calculation at the two endpoints gives the outward
 1342: certificate
 1343: \begin{equation}
 1344:  H\in[2.588435643856306896,2.589951928201414596].
 1345:  \label{eq:H-interval-certificate}
 1346: \end{equation}
 1347: 
 1348: Exact integer enumeration of \(\mathcal C(q)\) for \(2\le q\le20000\), with
 1349: only the powers \(q^{-s}\) evaluated by directed rounding, gives
 1350: \[
 1351:  \sum_{q=2}^{20000}\mathcal C(q)q^{-\sigma_0}
 1352:  \in[19.302273469878911856,19.311145544445741772].
 1353: \]
 1354: For the lower tail, fix \(1\le p\le7900\).  The elementary continued-fraction
 1355: identities for the symmetric residues imply, for \(q>20000\),
 1356: \[
 1357:  c(p/q)+c((q-p)/q)\ge\frac{4q}{p}-6.
 1358: \]
 1359: Indeed, \(p/q<1/2\), and replacing the initial partial quotient \(a_1\)
 1360: by \(1,a_1-1\) shows that \(S(p/q)=S((q-p)/q)\).  If \(p>1\), the first
 1361: Euclidean quotient is \(\lfloor q/p\rfloor\) and the remaining quotient sum
 1362: is at least \(2\); for \(p=1\), the quotient sum is \(q\).  These observations
 1363: give the displayed inequality (in fact, with room to spare).
 1364: The function
 1365: \(g_{p,s}(x)=4x^{1-s}/p-6x^{-s}\) is decreasing in the relevant range,
 1366: because
 1367: \[
 1368:  \frac{3(2.4787)(7900)}{2(2.4787-1)}<19864<20000.
 1369: \]
 1370: Every block of \(p\) consecutive integers contains exactly
 1371: \(\phi_{\rm E}(p)\) integers coprime to \(p\).  The selected residue pairs
 1372: are disjoint, and blockwise integration therefore gives
 1373: \[
 1374:  \begin{aligned}
 1375:  \sum_{q>20000}\mathcal C(q)q^{-\sigma_0}
 1376:  &\ge\sum_{p=1}^{7900}\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(p)}p
 1377:  \bigg\{\frac{4(20000+p)^{2-\sigma_0}}
 1378:                  {p(\sigma_0-2)}\\
 1379:  &\hspace{43mm}
 1380:  -\frac{6(20000+p)^{1-\sigma_0}}{\sigma_0-1}\bigg\}\\
 1381:  &\ge0.434421763138508560.
 1382:  \end{aligned}
 1383: \]
 1384: The integrand is positive and decreases as \(s\) increases, so the final
 1385: outward evaluation is made at \(s=2.4788\).
 1386: For the opposite direction, \eqref{eq:general-cost-sum-bound} and the
 1387: integral test yield, with
 1388: \(f_s(x)=x^{1-s}\{9+8(1+\log x)^3\}\),
 1389: \[
 1390:  \begin{aligned}
 1391:  \sum_{q>20000}\mathcal C(q)q^{-\sigma_0}
 1392:  &\le f_{2.4787}(20000)
 1393:        +\int_{20000}^{\infty}f_{2.4787}(x)\,dx\\
 1394:  &\le347.686170804099215772.
 1395:  \end{aligned}
 1396: \]
 1397: Here direct differentiation shows that \(f_s\) is decreasing for
 1398: \(s\ge2.4787\) and \(x\ge20000\); the largest upper integrand occurs at
 1399: \(s=2.4787\).
 1400: Consequently,
 1401: \begin{equation}
 1402:  D\in[19.736695233017420416,366.997316348544957544].
 1403:  \label{eq:D-interval-certificate}
 1404: \end{equation}
 1405: Equations \eqref{eq:H-interval-certificate} and
 1406: \eqref{eq:D-interval-certificate} prove
 1407: \eqref{eq:critical-speed-certificate}; subtraction gives
 1408: \eqref{eq:strict-speed-separation}.  All integer enumerations, analytic
 1409: remainders, and rounding directions in this certificate are reproduced by
 1410: the accompanying script \texttt{verify\_speed\_separation.py}.
 1411: 
 1412: Finally, \(\alpha<v_c<v_2\) implies
 1413: \[
 1414:  \frac{m}{r_m}\longrightarrow\frac{\log2}{\alpha}>\gamma_2.
 1415: \]
 1416: The finite-window assertion of \Cref{thm:dyadic-exact-multiplier} applies and
 1417: gives \eqref{eq:dyadic-uniform-speed-counterexample}.
 1418: \end{proof}
 1419: 
 1420: \begin{proposition}[Polynomial conditional upper tail]
 1421: \label{prop:conditional-polynomial-upper-tail}
 1422: Under the fixed-window hypotheses of
 1423: \Cref{thm:conditional-phase-separation}, there exist \(\delta>0\) and
 1424: \(c>0\) such that, for all sufficiently large \(m\),
 1425: \[
 1426:  \Pr\left\{
 1427:   \frac1mL(g_m(\mathbf X_{m,\alpha,\varepsilon}))
 1428:   -\frac{\alpha+\varepsilon}{v_c}>\delta
 1429:  \right\}
 1430:  \ge c m^{1-\sigma_0}.
 1431: \]
 1432: In particular, this upper deviation has exponential decay rate zero.
 1433: \end{proposition}
 1434: 
 1435: \begin{proof}
 1436: Retain \(b=\alpha+\varepsilon\) and \(r=1/v_c\), so that \(rb<1\).
 1437: Fix \(h>0\), and let \(Z_m\) denote the cardinality of the conditioning set.
 1438: Choose
 1439: \[
 1440:  0<\theta_0<\theta_1<\frac{1-rb}{2}
 1441: \]
 1442: and then \(\zeta>0\) sufficiently small that
 1443: \((r+\zeta)b+2\theta_1<1\) and
 1444: \(2\theta_0-\zeta b>0\).  For
 1445: \(\theta_0m\le q\le\theta_1m\), consider critical renewal words with
 1446: \[
 1447:  bm-\log q-2h<T\le bm-\log q-h,
 1448:  \qquad |S/T-r|\le\zeta.
 1449: \]
 1450: Equations \eqref{eq:blackwell-critical-renewal} and
 1451: \eqref{eq:marked-renewal-reward-law}, uniformly in this tail family of
 1452: intervals, show that their unweighted number is at least
 1453: \[
 1454:  c_0e^{\sigma_0bm}q^{-\sigma_0}
 1455: \]
 1456: for a fixed \(c_0>0\).  Append to each word the letter \(1/q\).  The free-word
 1457: decomposition makes all resulting words distinct, while
 1458: \[
 1459:  c(1/q)=2q-1,
 1460:  \qquad H(1/q)=\log q.
 1461: \]
 1462: Their total energies lie in \((bm-2h,bm-h]\).  By the choices of
 1463: \(\theta_1\) and \(\zeta\), their total costs are at most \(m-1\) for large
 1464: \(m\), so \(K_m=4\); by the choices of \(\theta_0\) and \(\zeta\), their
 1465: costs divided by \(m\) exceed \(rb\) by a fixed positive amount.
 1466: Consequently the exceptional-state count is at least
 1467: \[
 1468:  c_1e^{\sigma_0bm}
 1469:  \sum_{\theta_0m\le q\le\theta_1m}q^{-\sigma_0}
 1470:  \ge c_2e^{\sigma_0bm}m^{1-\sigma_0}.
 1471: \]
 1472: On the other hand, covering the whole conditioning interval by intervals of
 1473: length \(h\), using \(K_m\le4\) and the deterministic bound \(N_h\), gives
 1474: \(Z_m\le c_3e^{\sigma_0bm}\).  Division proves the asserted lower bound.
 1475: \end{proof}
 1476: 
 1477: \begin{remark}[Imported and new components]
 1478: Weinstein's free-monoid parameterization, multiplicativity of \(R(g)\), and
 1479: largest-index formula \eqref{eq:generator-weight-length} are published inputs.
 1480: The weighted cost \(c(p/q)\), the finite-layer renewal identity
 1481: \eqref{eq:weighted-layer-renewal}, its transfer to the finite-window sums, and
 1482: the all-real finite-window pressure formula are the additional conclusions
 1483: here.  The orbit thinning and parity-complete filling in
 1484: \Cref{thm:full-fiber-ldp} are the additional ingredients that close the
 1485: nonexposed large-deviation slopes.  The standard upper bound and the
 1486: exposed-slope tilting argument are not claimed as new.  The Stern--Brocot
 1487: multifractal theorem of Kesseb\"ohmer and Stratmann
 1488: \cite{KessebohmerStratmann2004,KessebohmerStratmann2007} concerns interval
 1489: growth rates and does not state the finite-window LDP above.  The
 1490: Stern--Brocot pressure and the Knauf
 1491: denominator-layer estimate
 1492: are cited published inputs.  In
 1493: particular, the argument does not reprove or invoke Weinstein's eventual
 1494: layer-stabilization theorem.  The marked identity
 1495: \eqref{eq:marked-microcanonical-count} is a direct bivariate restatement of
 1496: \Cref{prop:weighted-generator-renewal}, not an additional result.  The
 1497: classical Blackwell theorem is used only for the renewal limit
 1498: \eqref{eq:blackwell-critical-renewal}.  The finite-window conditional law, its
 1499: admissible shrinking-window form, and the polynomial conditional tail are
 1500: additional finite-window conclusions.  The dyadic word count in
 1501: \Cref{thm:dyadic-exact-multiplier} is a coefficient consequence of Weinstein's
 1502: published free-monoid multiplier formula; the conditional generator-cost
 1503: bridge and its exact finite-window transfer are the additional conclusions.
 1504: \end{remark}

The LDP proof then uses an orbit-filling injection to supply slopes that standard Gärtner–Ellis theory misses; this is more than raw computation, but its novelty depends heavily on whether that renewal reformulation is already implicit in the prior thermodynamic-formalism literature.

# Literature Check: Real-Tilt Pressure and Large Deviations

Date of search: 2026-08-02 (Asia/Singapore).

## Search protocol

The arXiv Atom API (`https://export.arxiv.org/api/query`) was queried directly.
Bibliographic metadata and DOI records were checked against arXiv metadata,
OpenAlex, publisher DOI records, and the manuscript's existing references. A
Crossref REST query was also attempted; the endpoint returned HTTP 429 during
this search, so no claim below depends on an unavailable Crossref response.

The principal arXiv queries and results were as follows.

1. `all:"Fibonacci partition"` returned 12 records. The directly relevant
   records were Kempton, *The Dynamics of the Fibonacci Partition Function*,
   arXiv:2311.06006; Sanna, *A note on the power sums of the number of
   Fibonacci partitions*, arXiv:2309.12724; Chow--Jones, *On the variance of
   the Fibonacci partition function*, arXiv:2308.15415; Chow--Slattery, *On
   Fibonacci partitions*, arXiv:2009.08222; and Weinstein, *Notes on Fibonacci
   Partitions*, arXiv:math/0307150.
2. `all:"Fibonacci partition" AND (all:pressure OR all:"large deviation")`
   returned 0 records.
3. `all:"Fibonacci partition function" AND (all:moment OR
   all:thermodynamic)` returned 0 records.
4. `all:"Thermodynamic formalism for countable Markov shifts"` returned four
   later records using Sarig's formalism, including Iommi--Yayama,
   arXiv:1106.0720, DOI 10.1088/0951-7715/25/1/165. The original Sarig papers
   are publisher-indexed rather than arXiv-indexed.
5. `au:Sarig AND all:"phase transitions"` returned 0 records. The original
   published phase-transition paper was separately verified by DOI.
6. `all:"Gartner-Ellis" AND all:"large deviations"` returned seven later
   applications/generalizations; the original Gartner and Ellis articles were
   separately verified by DOI.
7. `all:"Ruelle-Perron-Frobenius" AND all:analytic` returned Giulietti et al.,
   *The calculus of thermodynamical formalism*, arXiv:1508.01297, as a modern
   compact-state comparator.

These searches do not constitute an exhaustive mathematical-novelty proof.
They establish only that no arXiv record located by the stated direct queries
claims the present finite-window real-tilt pressure theorem or LDP.

## Exact references and use

- D. Ruelle, *Thermodynamic Formalism*, 2nd ed., Cambridge University Press,
  2004, DOI 10.1017/CBO9780511617546. This is cited for the classical compact,
  spectral-gap Ruelle--Perron--Frobenius paradigm. Its hypotheses are not
  asserted for the present multiplicity cocycle.
- O. M. Sarig, "Thermodynamic formalism for countable Markov shifts,"
  *Ergodic Theory Dynam. Systems* 19 (1999), 1565--1593,
  DOI 10.1017/S0143385799146820. This is cited for the countable-state
  recurrence/RPF framework.
- O. M. Sarig, "Phase transitions for countable Markov shifts,"
  *Comm. Math. Phys.* 217 (2001), 555--577,
  DOI 10.1007/s002200100367. This is cited to distinguish countable-state
  phase-transition behavior from globally analytic compact-state pressure.
- J. Gartner, "On large deviations from the invariant measure,"
  *Theory Probab. Appl.* 22 (1977), 24--39, DOI 10.1137/1122003; and
  R. S. Ellis, "Large deviations for a general class of random vectors,"
  *Ann. Probab.* 12 (1984), 1--12, DOI 10.1214/aop/1176993370. These are the
  original large-deviation references behind the Gartner--Ellis theorem.
- A. Dembo and O. Zeitouni, *Large Deviations Techniques and Applications*,
  2nd ed., Springer, 2010, DOI 10.1007/978-3-642-03311-7, Theorem 2.3.6. This
  is the precise modern theorem invoked for the conditional full LDP.
- C. Sanna, "A note on the power sums of the number of Fibonacci partitions,"
  *Discrete Analysis* 2025:2, DOI 10.19086/da.137601,
  arXiv:2309.12724. Sanna proves integer-power asymptotics and the integer
  zero-temperature limit; no noninteger pressure regularity is claimed there.
- F. V. Weinstein, "Notes on Fibonacci partitions," *Experimental
  Mathematics* 25 (2016), 482--499,
  DOI 10.1080/10586458.2015.1118416, arXiv:math/0307150. The manuscript uses
  Weinstein's Theorems 3.8, 5.1, and 10.3 for the generating-orbit level
  decomposition and the exact Dirichlet series
  `1 + sum Psi(k) k^{-s} = (2 - zeta(s-1)/zeta(s))^{-1}`. The published proof
  is cited, not reproduced.
- T. Kempton, "The Dynamics of the Fibonacci Partition Function,"
  arXiv:2311.06006. This provides a matrix-cocycle/irrational-rotation
  description of local Fibonacci-partition dynamics, but does not establish
  the finite-window real-moment pressure or its large deviations.

## Novelty conclusion and obstruction

The requested theorem, namely a finite all-real pressure that is real analytic
and strictly convex on the whole real line, is false. Weinstein's published
level-set orbit decomposition implies an exact frozen phase. If

`zeta(sigma_0 - 1) / zeta(sigma_0) = 2`, with
`sigma_0 = 2.478750785733960...`, then the manuscript proves

`P(t) = 0` for every `t <= -sigma_0`.

Since `P(0) = log(phi)`, analytic continuation from the frozen open half-line
would give a contradiction. This also excludes strict convexity on all of the
real line. The new, fully proved contribution is the transfer of Weinstein's
level-orbit Dirichlet structure through the paper's exact two-layer fiber
identity to obtain this finite-window freezing theorem, including the critical
endpoint. No located reference states this finite-window consequence.

The residual open interface is existence for every
`t in (-sigma_0, infinity)`, differentiability at `-sigma_0`, and analytic
positive curvature within the open positive-pressure phase. Consequently the
full LDP remains conditional on this corrected residual hypothesis. The paper
does not claim that general Ruelle or Sarig theory verifies the required
operator hypotheses for this cocycle.

å¯¹  $k\ge2$ ï¼Œæœ¬æ–‡åŠ å¼ºçš„é€å±‚ä¼°è®¡ç»™å‡º  $N_j(k)\le2\Psi(k)$ ï¼Œè€Œç¨³å®šåŒºé—´å†…  $N_j(k)=2\Psi(k)$ ã€‚ç›¸åŠ å³å¾—ç»“è®ºã€‚Weinstein å…¬å¼€å‘è¡¨çš„åŽŸå§‹ç»“æžœç»™å‡ºäº†å•å±‚ç¨³å®šåŠå…¶è½¨é“è§£é‡Šï¼›æœ¬æ–‡çš„æ—©å±‚åˆ†æžå°†å…¶å»¶ä¼¸è‡³æ‰€éœ€ç«¯ç‚¹ï¼Œè€Œä¸Šè¿°æœ‰é™çª—è°±æµ‹åº¦å¹¶æœªè§äºŽè¯¥æ–‡ã€‚Weinstein, Notes on Fibonacci Partitions
è¿™ä¸€å®šç†è¯´æ˜Žï¼šè™½ç„¶çº¤ç»´æ€»æ•°ä»¥  $\varphi^m$  å¢žé•¿ï¼Œæ¯ä¸ªå›ºå®šæœ‰é™é‡æ•°çš„ç»å¯¹ç®€å¹¶åº¦å´æœ€ç»ˆåœæ­¢å¢žé•¿ã€‚æœ‰é™çª—çš„ä½Žèƒ½è°±ä¸æ˜¯è¶‹å‘è¿žç»­å¯†åº¦ï¼Œè€Œæ˜¯è¶‹å‘ä¸€ä¸ªç”± Weinstein ç”Ÿæˆè½¨é“å®Œå…¨ç¼–ç çš„ç¦»æ•£æ— é™æµ‹åº¦ã€‚

å®šç†äºŒï¼šè´Ÿæ¸©é…åˆ†å‡½æ•°çš„æ˜¾å¼æžé™åŠå…¶ç²¾ç¡®æ”¶æ•›è¾¹ç•Œ
ä»¤  $\sigma _0>2$  ç”±

$$\frac{\zeta(\sigma _0-1)}{\zeta(\sigma _0)}=2$$

å”¯ä¸€ç¡®å®šã€‚å¯¹  $s>\sigma _0$ ï¼Œæœ‰éžæŒ‡æ•°å½’ä¸€åŒ–çš„å¼ºæžé™

$$\boxed{\ 
\lim_{m\to\infty}S_{-s}(m)
 =\mathcal Z(s)
 :=\frac{4}{\,2-\zeta(s-1)/\zeta(s)\,}-2.
\ }$$

è€Œä¸”ï¼Œå¯¹ä»»æ„å®žæ•°  $t$ ï¼Œ

$$\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
t<-\sigma _0&\Longrightarrow S_t(m)\longrightarrow
 \frac{4}{\,2-\zeta(-t-1)/\zeta(-t)\,}-2<\infty,\\
t\ge-\sigma _0&\Longrightarrow S_t(m)\longrightarrow+\infty.
\end{aligned}}$$

å› æ­¤ï¼Œ $-\sigma _0$  è™½æœªå¿…æ˜¯é›¶åŽ‹åŠ›åŒºé—´çš„çœŸå®žç«¯ç‚¹ï¼Œå´æ˜¯æœªç»æŒ‡æ•°å½’ä¸€åŒ–çš„æœ‰é™çª—é…åˆ†å‡½æ•°çš„ç²¾ç¡®æ”¶æ•›ä¸´ç•Œç‚¹ã€‚
è¯æ˜Ž
ç”±å®šç†ä¸€åŠæ”¯é…æ”¶æ•›ï¼Œ

$$S_{-s}(m)=\sum_{k\ge1}C_m(k)k^{-s}
 \longrightarrow 2+4\sum_{k\ge2}\frac{\Psi(k)}{k^s},
\qquad s>\sigma _0.$$

Weinstein çš„è½¨é“ Dirichlet çº§æ•°ä¸º

$$1+\sum_{k\ge2}\frac{\Psi(k)}{k^s}
 =\left(2-\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}\right)^{-1},$$

ä»Žè€Œå¾—åˆ°æ˜¾å¼å…¬å¼ã€‚
è‹¥  $0<s\le\sigma _0$ ï¼Œä¸Šè¿°  $\Psi$ -çº§æ•°å‘æ•£ã€‚ä»»å–  $K$ ï¼Œå½“  $m\ge2K$  æ—¶ï¼Œ

$$S_{-s}(m)\ge
2+4\sum_{2\le k\le K}\Psi(k)k^{-s}.$$

ä»¤  $K\to\infty$  å³å¾—  $S_{-s}(m)\to\infty$ ã€‚å½“  $t\ge0$  æ—¶ï¼Œç”±  $d_m(x)\ge1$ ï¼Œ

$$S_t(m)\ge |X_m|=F_{m+2}\to\infty.$$

è¯æ¯•ã€‚

å®šç†ä¸‰ï¼šæžé™ Gibbs è°±åŠä¸€çº§ä¸´ç•Œæžç‚¹
ç½®

$$\kappa:=
-\left.\frac{d}{ds}\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}
  \right|_{s=\sigma _0}
=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\varphi_{\!E}(n)\log n}{n^{\sigma _0}}
>0.$$

æ•°å€¼ä¸Šï¼Œ

$$\kappa=2.58918437999\ldots .$$

å½“  $s\downarrow\sigma _0$  æ—¶ï¼Œ

$$\boxed{\ 
\mathcal Z(s)
 =\frac{4}{\kappa(s-\sigma _0)}+O(1).
\ }$$

æ•…è¯¥ä¸´ç•Œç‚¹å…·æœ‰ä¸¥æ ¼çš„ä¸€é˜¶æžç‚¹ï¼Œä¸´ç•ŒæŒ‡æ•°ä¸º  $1$ ã€‚
è¿›ä¸€æ­¥ï¼Œå®šä¹‰è´Ÿæ¸© Gibbs æµ‹åº¦åœ¨çº¤ç»´é‡æ•°ä¸Šçš„æŽ¨å‰

$$Q_{m,s}(k):=
\frac{C_m(k)k^{-s}}{S_{-s}(m)}.$$

å¯¹æ¯ä¸ª  $s>\sigma _0$ ï¼Œ $Q_{m,s}$  ä¾å…¨å˜å·®æ”¶æ•›äºŽ

$$\boxed{

\quad(k\ge2).
}$$

åœ¨ä¸´ç•Œç‚¹é™„è¿‘ï¼Œ

$$Q_s(1)\sim \frac{\kappa}{2}(s-\sigma _0),$$

å¹¶ä¸”è‹¥  $K$  è¡¨ç¤ºæžé™ Gibbs è°±ä¸‹çš„çº¤ç»´é‡æ•°ï¼Œåˆ™

$$\mathbb E_s[\log K]

\qquad

   =\frac1{(s-\sigma _0)^2}+O(1).$$

è¯æ˜Ž
ç”±äºŽ

$$\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}
 =\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\varphi_{\!E}(n)}{n^s},$$

å…¶å¯¼æ•°åœ¨  $s=\sigma _0>2$  å¤„ä¸¥æ ¼ä¸ºè´Ÿã€‚å› æ­¤

$$2-\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}

ä»£å…¥å®šç†äºŒå³å¾—æžç‚¹å±•å¼€ã€‚å…¨å˜å·®æ”¶æ•›ç”±

$$C_m(k)k^{-s}\le4\Psi(k)k^{-s}$$

åŠå¯æ±‚å’Œæ”¯é…å‡½æ•°æŽ¨å‡ºã€‚æœ€åŽï¼Œ

$$-\frac{d}{ds}\log\mathcal Z(s)=\mathbb E_s[\log K],
\qquad

å†å¯¹æžç‚¹å±•å¼€æ±‚å¯¼å³å¯ã€‚
å…¶å«ä¹‰åœ¨äºŽï¼šæœ¬æ–‡å·²ç»è¯æ˜Žçš„é›¶åŽ‹åŠ›åŠç›´çº¿å†…éƒ¨å¹¶éžçƒ­åŠ›å­¦ä¸Šå®Œå…¨å¹³å‡¡ã€‚æŒ‡æ•°åŽ‹åŠ›æ’ç­‰äºŽé›¶ï¼Œä½†æœªç»æŒ‡æ•°å½’ä¸€åŒ–çš„è°±é…åˆ†å‡½æ•°åœ¨  $\sigma _0$  å¤„å‘ç”Ÿå…·æœ‰æ˜¾å¼ä¸´ç•ŒæŒ‡æ•°çš„ç›¸å˜ï¼›è¯¥çŽ°è±¡ä¸èƒ½ç”±æœ¬æ–‡çŽ°æœ‰çš„åŽ‹åŠ›ç»“è®ºè§‚å¯Ÿåˆ°ã€‚

å®šç†å››ï¼šç”Ÿæˆè½¨é“æ•°çš„ Tauber åž‹æ¸è¿‘
æœ‰

$$\boxed{\

 \sim \frac{x^{\sigma _0}}{\sigma _0\kappa}.

ä¸´ç•Œä¸‹ç•Œå–å·²ç¨³å®šçš„  $k\le m/2$ ï¼›ä¸Šç•Œä½¿ç”¨  $k\le D_m$  ä¸Ž

Weinstein ç»™å‡ºäº† Dirichlet çº§æ•°ï¼Œä½†æ£€ç´¢åˆ°çš„å…¬å¼€ç‰ˆæœ¬æœªç»™å‡ºè¿™ä¸€å…³äºŽ  $\Psi(k)$  çš„ Tauber åž‹å¢žé•¿å¾‹ã€‚Weinstein åŽŸæ–‡

å®šç†äº”ï¼šé›¶åŽ‹åŠ›è½¬å˜åŒºé—´å¯ç”±  $0.4787$  åŽ‹ç¼©è‡³  $0.2082$

$$p_m(t):=\frac1m\log S_t(m),\qquad
\beta_*:=\frac{\log\varphi}{\log(2/\varphi)}
       =2.27055945396\ldots .$$

å¯¹  $0<s<\sigma _0$ ï¼Œ

å¦ä¸€æ–¹é¢ï¼Œä»»å–  $a>\sigma _0$ ã€‚ç”±å®šç†ä¸€ï¼Œ

è¯¥ç»“æžœä¸è§£å†³éžæ•´æ•°å®žå€¾æ–œä¸‹åŽ‹åŠ›æžé™çš„å­˜åœ¨æ€§æˆ–è§£æžæ€§ï¼›å®ƒæ— æ¡ä»¶è¯æ˜Žçš„æ˜¯ï¼šåœ¨  $t>-\beta_*$  ä¸Šï¼Œä»»ä½•æžé™æˆ–å­åºåˆ—æžé™å‡ä¸å¯èƒ½ä¸ºé›¶ã€‚

å®šç†å…­ï¼šæœ‰é™çª—æŠ˜å çš„ç²¾ç¡®ä¿¡æ¯æŸå¤±çŽ‡

æ•…æŠ˜å æ˜ å°„è™½å¯¹å…¨éƒ¨  $F_{m+2}$  ä¸ªåˆæ³•å­—æ»¡å°„ï¼Œå…¶è¾“å‡ºåœ¨  $L^2$  æ„ä¹‰ä¸‹å¹¶ä¸æ¸è¿‘å‡åŒ€ï¼Œè€Œæ˜¯ä»¥ä¸¥æ ¼æ­£æŒ‡æ•°åç¦»å‡åŒ€åˆ†å¸ƒã€‚

$$\boxed{\kappa=2.589184379946924126\ldots } .$$

$$U_s(z)=\frac1{1-B_s(z)}$$

åŠ (5)ï¼Œå½“  $s<\sigma _0$  æ—¶ï¼Œ $U_s$  çš„ç²¾ç¡®æ”¶æ•›åŠå¾„ä¸º  $r_s$ ã€‚
å½“  $s=\sigma _0$  æ—¶ï¼Œ $B_s(1)=1$ ï¼Œæ•…åŠå¾„ä¸º  $1$ ã€‚å½“
 $s>\sigma _0$  æ—¶ï¼Œ

$$U_s(1)=\frac1{1-B_s(1)}<\infty,$$

æ‰€ä»¥å…¶åŠå¾„è‡³å°‘ä¸º  $1$ ï¼›å¦ä¸€æ–¹é¢ï¼Œä¸€å­—æ¯è¯å·²ç»ç»™å‡º
 $u_j(s)\ge b_j(s)$ ï¼Œæ•…  $U_s$  çš„åŠå¾„ä¸å¯èƒ½è¶…è¿‡  $B_s$  çš„åŠå¾„  $1$ ã€‚äºŽæ˜¯

$$R_U(s)=
\begin{cases}
r_s,&s<\sigma _0,\\

\end{cases}
\tag{6}$$

å­—æ¯ä»£ä»·  $3,5$  å‡å‡ºçŽ°ï¼Œå› æ­¤  $u_j(s)>0$  å¯¹æ‰€æœ‰  $j\ge8$  æˆç«‹ã€‚è¯è¿žæŽ¥ç»™å‡º

$$u_{m+n}(s)\ge u_m(s)u_n(s).$$

å¯¹è¿™ä¸€ä½™æœ‰é™æ”¯æŒåº”ç”¨ç§»ä½åŽçš„ Fekete è®ºè¯ï¼Œå¾—åˆ°çœŸæ­£çš„æžé™è€Œéžä»…æœ‰ä¸Šæžé™ï¼š

$$\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac1j\log u_j(s)
 =-\log R_U(s).
\tag{7}$$

4. å‘æœ‰é™çª—çš„åŒå‘æŒ‡æ•°ä¼ é€’
ä»¤

$$H_m(s)=\sum_{j=0}^m u_j(s).$$

ä¿®æ­£åŽçš„æœ‰é™çª—æ’ç­‰å¼ç›´æŽ¥ç»™å‡ºï¼Œå¯¹æ¯ä¸ª  $m\ge1$ ï¼Œ

$$\boxed{\;

\;}
\tag{8}$$

ç”± (7) åŠ  $R_U(s)\le1$ ï¼Œ

$$\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log H_m(s)
 =-\log R_U(s).$$

å†ç”± (8)ï¼Œ

$$\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_{-s}(m)
 =-\log R_U(s),$$

ä»Žè€Œå¾—åˆ°å…¨å®žå€¾æ–œå…¬å¼ã€‚
5. è§£æžæ€§ä¸Žä¸¥æ ¼å‡¸æ€§
å–  $t>-\sigma _0$ ï¼Œå¹¶å®šä¹‰å­—æ¯æ¦‚çŽ‡

$$\mu_t(p/q)
 :=q^t e^{-P(t)c(p/q)}.$$

æ ¹æ–¹ç¨‹è¯´æ˜Žå…¶æ€»è´¨é‡ä¸º  $1$ ã€‚ç”±äºŽæ ¹ä¸¥æ ¼ä½äºŽ  $B_{-t}$  çš„æ”¶æ•›åœ†å†…ï¼Œå­˜åœ¨æŒ‡æ•°ä½™é‡ï¼›ç»“åˆ

$$\log q\ll d(p/q),\qquad c(p/q)=2d(p/q)+1,$$

å¯çŸ¥ä»»æ„é˜¶çš„  $t$ -å¯¼æ•°åŠ  $P$ -å¯¼æ•°å‡å±€éƒ¨ä¸€è‡´æ”¶æ•›ã€‚è§£æžéšå‡½æ•°å®šç†å› è€Œé€‚ç”¨ï¼Œä¸æ¶‰åŠä»»ä½•å¾…éªŒè¯çš„ç®—å­å‡è®¾ã€‚

$$P'(t)=\frac{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}X}{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}C},

$$P''(t)

åˆ†æ•°  $1/2$  ä¸Ž  $1/3$  åˆ†åˆ«å…·æœ‰  $(C,q)=(3,2)$  ä¸Ž

æ•… (10) ä¸¥æ ¼ä¸ºæ­£ï¼Œè¯æ˜Žå®žè§£æžæ€§ä¸Žä¸¥æ ¼å‡¸æ€§ã€‚
6. ä¸´ç•Œå•ä¾§å¯¼æ•°

$$\frac pq=[0;a_1,\ldots ,a_r]$$

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Regression tests for the strict speed-separation certificate."""

from fractions import Fraction

import verify_speed_separation as certificate


def test_dyadic_tail_certificate_matches_exact_rational_values() -> None:
    partial = sum(
        (
            Fraction(certificate.dyadic_cost(exponent), 3 ** (exponent + 1))
            for exponent in range(1, 26)
        ),
        Fraction(0),
    )
    tail = certificate.GAMMA_TAIL
    upper = certificate.GAMMA_UPPER
    assert partial == Fraction(13_180_988_392_373, 2_541_865_828_329)
    assert tail == Fraction(126_600_871_936, 2_541_865_828_329)
    assert upper == Fraction(4_435_863_088_103, 847_288_609_443)


def test_complete_block_cost_identity_on_a_nontrivial_range() -> None:
    certificate.verify_complete_block_identity(max_denominator=250)


def test_certified_speed_gap_exceeds_oracle_claim() -> None:
    assert certificate.main() == 0

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Unit tests for the all-real pressure verification helpers."""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
import unittest

import verify_real_tilt_pressure as verification
from verify_real_tilt_pressure import (
    coexistence_local_counterexample_search,
    critical_slope_partial,
    negative_continued_fraction_value,
    orbit_padding_counterexample_search,
    regular_partial_quotient_sum,
    stern_brocot_layer_denominators,
)


class AllRealPressureHelpersTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dyadic_exact_multiplier_counts_match_direct_fibers(self) -> None:
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(verification, "dyadic_generator_cost_counters"))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(verification, "dyadic_finite_window_count"))
        counters = verification.dyadic_generator_cost_counters(6)
        for exponent in range(1, 7):
            self.assertEqual(sum(counters[exponent].values()), 3 ** (exponent - 1))

        fibonacci = [0, 1]
        for _ in range(24):
            fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
        coefficients = [1]
        for m in range(1, 21):
            coefficients = verification.extend_coefficients(coefficients, fibonacci[m])
            direct = verification.Counter(coefficients)
            for exponent in range(2, 7):
                self.assertEqual(
                    verification.dyadic_finite_window_count(m, counters[exponent]),
                    direct[2**exponent],
                )

    def test_conditional_marked_scan_matches_direct_band_counts(self) -> None:
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(verification, "conditional_marked_scan"))
        fibonacci = [0, 1]
        for _ in range(20):
            fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
        generators = verification.weighted_generator_counters(18)
        rows = verification.conditional_marked_scan(
            (12, 16), generators, ((0.08, 0.02),)
        )
        coefficients = [1]
        direct_counts = {}
        for m in range(1, 17):
            coefficients = verification.extend_coefficients(coefficients, fibonacci[m])
            if m in (12, 16):
                direct_counts[m] = sum(
                    1
                    for level in coefficients
                    if abs(math.log(level) / m - 0.08) < 0.02
                )
        self.assertEqual([row[3] for row in rows], [direct_counts[12], direct_counts[16]])
        self.assertTrue(all(0.06 < row[4] < 0.10 for row in rows))
        self.assertTrue(all(0.0 < row[5] <= 1.0 for row in rows))

    def test_marked_window_counter_recovers_exact_fiber_spectrum(self) -> None:
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(verification, "marked_window_counter"))
        fibonacci = [0, 1]
        for _ in range(16):
            fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
        generators = verification.weighted_generator_counters(13)
        coefficients = [1]
        for m in range(1, 13):
            coefficients = verification.extend_coefficients(coefficients, fibonacci[m])
            if m < 4:
                continue
            marked = verification.marked_window_counter(m, generators)
            predicted = verification.Counter()
            for (cost, level), count in marked.items():
                self.assertLessEqual(cost, m + 1)
                predicted[level] += count
            actual = verification.Counter(coefficients)
            del actual[1]
            self.assertEqual(predicted, actual)

    def test_regular_and_negative_costs_agree(self) -> None:
        examples = {
            (1,): (1, 2),
            (2,): (1, 3),
            (1, 1): (2, 3),
            (1, 2): (3, 5),
        }
        for composition, fraction in examples.items():
            self.assertEqual(
                negative_continued_fraction_value(composition), fraction
            )
            numerator, denominator = fraction
            self.assertEqual(
                regular_partial_quotient_sum(numerator, denominator) - 1,
                sum(composition),
            )

    def test_layer_counts_and_denominator_extrema(self) -> None:
        fibonacci = [0, 1]
        for _ in range(10):
            fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
        for depth in range(1, 9):
            denominators = stern_brocot_layer_denominators(depth)
            self.assertEqual(len(denominators), 2 ** (depth - 1))
            self.assertGreaterEqual(min(denominators), depth + 1)
            self.assertLessEqual(max(denominators), fibonacci[depth + 2])
            self.assertEqual(
                sum(denominators), 2 * 3 ** (depth - 1)
            )

    def test_truncated_critical_slope_is_finite_and_positive(self) -> None:
        numerator, denominator, slope = critical_slope_partial(
            2.4787507857339603, 100
        )
        self.assertTrue(math.isfinite(numerator))
        self.assertTrue(math.isfinite(denominator))
        self.assertTrue(math.isfinite(slope))
        self.assertGreater(numerator, 0.0)
        self.assertGreater(denominator, 0.0)
        self.assertGreater(slope, 0.0)

    def test_orbit_capacity_and_padding_have_no_small_counterexamples(self) -> None:
        fibonacci = [0, 1]
        for _ in range(18):
            fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
        failures, capacity_checks, padding_checks = (
            orbit_padding_counterexample_search(12, 16, fibonacci)
        )
        self.assertEqual(failures, [])
        self.assertGreater(capacity_checks, 0)
        self.assertGreater(padding_checks, 0)

    def test_coexistence_local_critical_bound_has_no_small_counterexamples(
        self,
    ) -> None:
        fibonacci = [0, 1]
        for _ in range(22):
            fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
        failures, checks, rows = coexistence_local_counterexample_search(
            (12, 16, 20),
            fibonacci,
            2.4787507857339603,
            ((0.04, 0.02), (0.08, 0.02), (0.12, 0.02)),
        )
        self.assertEqual(failures, [])
        self.assertEqual(checks, 9)
        self.assertEqual(len(rows), checks)
        self.assertTrue(all(row[3] > 0 for row in rows))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

$$\boxed{\kappa=2.589184379946924126\ldots } .$$

稿件中所有依赖  $\kappa$  的数值系数相应为
系数正确数值 $4/\kappa$  $1.544888046977171\ldots$  $\log(4/\kappa)$  $0.434951446220798\ldots$  $\kappa/2$  $1.294592189973462063\ldots$  $1/\kappa$  $0.386222011744293\ldots$  $1/(\sigma_0\kappa)$  $0.155813167651677\ldots$  $2\log\varphi/\kappa$  $0.371709198299325\ldots$ 
因此 Theorems 5.3–5.4 中的符号公式保持不变，但相应数值展开须采用上表。以下证明不调用 Weinstein 的层稳定定理；尤其不把  $j=2k$  归入其公开定理。该端点仍是本稿利用最大生成元引理所得的独立加强。
定理：实负倾斜压力的精确更新方程
令  $s=-t$ 。对每个

$$s\in[\beta_*,\sigma_0],$$

极限

$$P(-s)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_{-s}(m)$$

存在。更精确地，令  $p/q\in(0,1)$  为既约分数，并写出其唯一负连分数

$$\frac pq=
\cfrac1{a_1-\cfrac1{a_2-\ddots-\cfrac1{a_r}}},
\qquad a_i\ge2.$$

定义

$$d(p/q):=\sum_{i=1}^r(a_i-1),\qquad
c(p/q):=2d(p/q)+1.$$

则  $P(-s)$  是方程

$$\boxed{\;
\sum_{q=2}^{\infty}
\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
q^{-s}\exp\!\bigl(-P(-s)c(p/q)\bigr)=1
\;}
\tag{1}$$

的唯一非负解。因而

$$P(-\sigma_0)=0,\qquad
P(-s)>0\quad(\beta_*\le s<\sigma_0).$$

故零压力集合的右端点精确为

$$\boxed{t_c=-\sigma_0
=-2.478750785733960\ldots } .$$

证明
记

$$I_j=[F_{j+1}-1,F_{j+2}-1),\qquad
\Lambda_j(s):=\sum_{n\in I_j}R(n)^{-s}.$$

本稿的精确双层恒等式给出

$$S_{-s}(m)=\Lambda_m(s)+\Lambda_{m+1}(s).
\tag{2}$$

1. 长度标记的自由幺半群
对向量  $\mathbf a=(a_1,\ldots ,a_r)$ ，令

$$D(\varnothing)=1,\quad D(a_1)=a_1,$$


$$D(a_1,\ldots ,a_r)
=a_rD(a_1,\ldots ,a_{r-1})
-D(a_1,\ldots ,a_{r-2}).
\tag{3}$$

若  $\mathbf a$  是  $p/q$  的上述负连分数，则  $D(\mathbf a)=q$ 。
使用 Weinstein 的自由幺半群参数化，每个生成元  $g$  唯一对应于一个非空有序字

$$w=\frac{p_1}{q_1}\times\cdots\times\frac{p_r}{q_r},$$

并且

$$R(g)=\prod_{i=1}^r q_i,\qquad
L(g)=\sum_{i=1}^r c(p_i/q_i).
\tag{4}$$

这里只使用其生成元双射、分母乘法性及显式  $H$ -作用公式，而不使用层稳定定理；参见 Weinstein, §§3–4。
定义单字母权

$$b_\ell(s):=
\sum_{\substack{0<p<q,\ (p,q)=1\\c(p/q)=\ell}}q^{-s}.
\tag{5}$$

固定  $\ell$  时该和有限。事实上，若  $\ell=2d+1$ ，则

$$b_{2d+1}(s)=
\sum_{r=1}^{d}
\ \sum_{\substack{e_1+\cdots+e_r=d\\e_i\ge1}}
D(e_1+1,\ldots ,e_r+1)^{-s},
\qquad b_{2d}(s)=0.
\tag{6}$$

故这些系数可由有限组合和递推式 (3) 独立计算。
令

$$u_0(s)=1,\qquad
u_j(s)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}b_\ell(s)u_{j-\ell}(s).
\tag{7}$$

由自由字的唯一分解，

$$u_j(s)
=\sum_{\substack{g\text{ 为生成元}\\L(g)=j}}R(g)^{-s}.
\tag{8}$$

相应生成函数满足严格的形式恒等式

$$B_s(z):=\sum_{\ell\ge1}b_\ell(s)z^\ell,
\qquad
U_s(z):=\sum_{j\ge0}u_j(s)z^j
=\frac1{1-B_s(z)}.
\tag{9}$$

2. 更新方程精确表示有限窗和
每个生成元  $g$  属于 Weinstein 的奇首指标分支，故

$$L([a]g)=L(g)+2a,\qquad
L([a]\tau g)=L(g)+1+2a,$$

以及

$$L(\sigma n)=L(n)+1.
\tag{10}$$

又因  $R(n)>1$  时

$$n\in I_j\iff L(n)=j,$$

且  $H$ -作用自由、不同生成元的轨道不交，所以生成元  $g$  的整个轨道在  $I_j$  中贡献的点数恰为

$$\begin{cases}
0,&j<L(g),\\
1,&j=L(g),\\
2,&j>L(g).
\end{cases}
\tag{11}$$

每个  $I_j$  还恰有一个  $R(n)=1$  的左端点。因此

$$\boxed{\;
\Lambda_j(s)
=1+u_j(s)+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}u_\ell(s)
=2\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}u_\ell(s)+u_j(s)-1.
\;}
\tag{12}$$

结合 (2)，得到本文有限窗和的精确更新表示

$$\boxed{\;
S_{-s}(m)
=4\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_j(s)+3u_m(s)+u_{m+1}(s)-2.
\;}
\tag{13}$$

这不是层计数类比或上下界，而是逐轨道、逐有限窗的恒等式。
3. 更新方程的指数率存在
自由字连接给出

$$u_{m+n}(s)\ge u_m(s)u_n(s).
\tag{14}$$

字母  $1/2$  与  $1/3$  的代价分别为  $3,5$ ，故  $u_j(s)>0$  对所有  $j\ge8$  成立。于是尾序列  $\log u_j(s)$  超可加。对任意固定  $k\ge8$ ，把充分大的  $N$  写成

$$N=qk+r,\qquad 8\le r\le k+7,$$

由 (14) 得

$$\log u_N(s)\ge q\log u_k(s)+\log u_r(s).$$

因此广义 Fekete 论证给出极限

$$\gamma_s:=\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac1j\log u_j(s).
\tag{15}$$

另一方面，因为  $s>2$ ，

$$B_s(1)
=\sum_{q\ge2}\frac{\varphi_{\!E}(q)}{q^s}
=\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}-1<\infty.
\tag{16}$$

令  $r_s\in(0,1]$  为  $B_s(r_s)=1$  的解。对  $0\le z<r_s$ ，有  $B_s(z)<1$ ，故  $U_s(z)<\infty$ ；而在  $z=r_s$  处，

$$U_s(r_s)=\sum_{k\ge0}B_s(r_s)^k=+\infty.$$

所以  $U_s$  的收敛半径正是  $r_s$ 。由 Cauchy–Hadamard 公式及 (15)，

$$\gamma_s=-\log r_s.
\tag{17}$$

令  $H_j(s)=\sum_{\ell=0}^ju_\ell(s)$ 。由 (15)，

$$\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac1j\log H_j(s)=\gamma_s.
\tag{18}$$

而 (12) 给出

$$H_{j-1}(s)\le\Lambda_j(s)\le2H_j(s).$$

故

$$H_m(s)\le S_{-s}(m)\le4H_{m+1}(s).
\tag{19}$$

对数除以  $m$  并令  $m\to\infty$ ，由 (18) 得

$$P(-s)=\gamma_s=-\log r_s.$$

将  $r_s=e^{-P(-s)}$  代入  $B_s(r_s)=1$ ，即得方程 (1)。
4. 相变点
由 (16) 及  $\sigma_0$  的定义，

$$B_{\sigma_0}(1)=1.$$

所以  $r_{\sigma_0}=1$ ，从而

$$P(-\sigma_0)=0.$$

若  $\beta_*\le s<\sigma_0$ ，则逐项严格单调性给出

$$B_s(1)>B_{\sigma_0}(1)=1.$$

由于  $B_s(0)=0$  且  $B_s$  在  $[0,1]$  上连续严格递增，唯一根满足

$$0<r_s<1,$$

故

$$P(-s)=-\log r_s>0.$$

结合本稿已证明的  $P(t)=0$  对所有  $t\le-\sigma_0$  成立，遂得

$$\{t\le-\beta_*:P(t)=0\}=(-\infty,-\sigma_0],$$

尤其

$$\boxed{t_c=-\sigma_0}.$$

 succeeded in 166ms:
校正后的更新恒等式为

$$\Lambda _0(s)=0,
\qquad
\Lambda_j(s)
 =1+u_j(s)+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}u_\ell(s)
 =2\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}u_\ell(s)+u_j(s)-1,
\quad j\ge1.$$

有限窗恒等式对全部  $m\ge1$  保持不变。以下仅处理全实倾斜问题。
全实倾斜压力定理
令

$$a_d(s):=b_{2d+1}(s),\qquad d\ge1,$$

并以  $\mathfrak P(\theta)$  表示 Stern–Brocot 压力

$$\mathfrak P(\theta)
 =\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac1d
   \log\sum_{T\in\mathcal T_d}|T|^\theta .$$

其已发表性质为： $\mathfrak P$  在  $(-\infty,1)$  上实解析且严格为正，在  $[1,\infty)$  上恒等于零。本文仅调用这一既有输入及其领先本征值估计，不主张其优先权。Kesseböhmer–Stratmann 与 Fiala–Kleban–Özlük 给出了所需的 Stern–Brocot/Knauf 对应及定量压力结论。
则有：


 $B_s$  的收敛半径为

$$\boxed{\;
R_B(s)=\exp\!\left(-\frac12\mathfrak P(s/2)\right).
\;}$$

因而

$$R_B(s)<1\quad(s<2),\qquad R_B(s)=1\quad(s\ge2).$$



设  $\sigma _0>2$  是

$$\frac{\zeta(\sigma _0-1)}{\zeta(\sigma _0)}=2$$

的唯一解。方程  $B_s(r)=1$  的非负实根结构为

$$\begin{array}{c|c}
s<\sigma _0 & \text{唯一根 }r_s\in(0,R_B(s))\\
s=\sigma _0 & \text{唯一根 }r_{\sigma _0}=1=R_B(\sigma _0)\\
s>\sigma _0 & [0,R_B(s)]\text{ 内无根}.
\end{array}$$



对每个  $s\in\mathbb R$ ，极限

$$\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_{-s}(m)$$

均存在，并且

$$\boxed{
P(-s)=
\begin{cases}
-\log r_s,&s<\sigma _0,\\[2mm]
0,&s\ge\sigma _0.
\end{cases}}$$

等价地，若  $t>-\sigma _0$ ，则  $P(t)>0$  是方程

$$\boxed{\;
\sum_{q\ge2}\ \sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
     q^t e^{-P(t)c(p/q)}=1
\;}$$

的唯一正解；若  $t\le-\sigma _0$ ，则  $P(t)=0$ 。故最大正压力相恰为

$$\boxed{(-\sigma _0,\infty)}.$$



 $P$  在  $(-\sigma _0,\infty)$  上实解析且严格凸；但在
 $t_c=-\sigma _0$  处不可微，左右导数分别为

$$P'_-(t_c)=0,\qquad
P'_+(t_c)=
\frac{\displaystyle
  \sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{(p,q)=1}q^{-\sigma _0}\log q}
{\displaystyle
  \sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{(p,q)=1}c(p/q)q^{-\sigma _0}}
>0.$$

因而临界点具有两个有限单侧导数，但不是  $C^1$  接合。


证明
1. 字母层的指数增长与  $B_s$  的半径
负连分数参数化给出

$$a_d(s)=
\sum_{\substack{0<p<q,\ (p,q)=1\\d(p/q)=d}}q^{-s}.$$

深度为  $d$  的分数共有  $2^{d-1}$  个，并满足

$$d+1\le q\le F_{d+2}.$$

因此

$$\begin{aligned}
2^{d-1}F_{d+2}^{-s}
 &\le a_d(s)\le2^{d-1}(d+1)^{-s},
 &&s\ge0,\\
2^{d-1}(d+1)^{-s}
 &\le a_d(s)\le2^{d-1}F_{d+2}^{-s},
 &&s\le0.
\end{aligned}
\tag{1}$$

特别地，每个  $B_s$  均具有正且有限的收敛半径。
该深度层恰为 Knauf 模型的第  $d$  个新分母层。已发表的领先本征值/Gibbs 估计给出，对每个固定的  $s<2$ ，存在常数
 $0<C_s^-\le C_s^+<\infty$ ，使得

$$C_s^-e^{d\mathfrak P(s/2)}
 \le a_d(s)
 \le C_s^+e^{d\mathfrak P(s/2)}.
\tag{2}$$

在  $s\ge2$  时，

$$\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac1d\log a_d(s)=0.
\tag{3}$$

由于

$$B_s(z)=z\sum_{d\ge1}a_d(s)z^{2d},$$

Cauchy–Hadamard 公式立即给出

$$R_B(s)=e^{-\mathfrak P(s/2)/2}.$$

当  $s<2$  且  $|z|=R_B(s)$  时，由 (2)

$$|a_d(s)z^{2d+1}|\asymp_s1,$$

故通项不趋于零，边界上处处发散。对  $s=2$ ， $R_B(2)=1$ ，且正实边界发散。对  $s>2$ ，则

$$B_s(1)
 =\sum_{q\ge2}\frac{\varphi(q)}{q^s}
 =\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}-1<\infty,
\tag{4}$$

故单位圆上绝对收敛。由此完成所需边界分类。
2.  $B_s(r)=1$  的根
当  $s\le2$  时，正实边界值为  $+\infty$ ；当  $2<s<\sigma _0$  时，(4) 给出  $B_s(1)>1$ 。由于系数非负且  $b_3(s)>0$ ， $B_s(r)$  在正实收敛区间严格递增，故在这两种情形均存在唯一内部根。
当  $s=\sigma _0$  时， $B_s(1)=1$ 。当  $s>\sigma _0$  时，
 $B_s(1)<1$ ，故不存在非负实根。
此外，若  $s<\sigma _0$  且  $B_s(r_s)=1$ ，则对
 $|z|\le r_s$ 

$$|B_s(z)|\le B_s(|z|)\le1.$$

等号要求所有非零项具有同一相位。因支持集包含  $3$  与  $5$ ，其最大公因数为  $1$ ，故

$$B_s(z)=1,\quad |z|\le r_s
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad z=r_s.
\tag{5}$$

因此  $1-B_s$  在最小模圆上仅有单根  $r_s$ 。
3. 更新序列的精确半径
由形式恒等式

$$U_s(z)=\frac1{1-B_s(z)}$$

及 (5)，当  $s<\sigma _0$  时， $U_s$  的精确收敛半径为  $r_s$ 。
当  $s=\sigma _0$  时， $B_s(1)=1$ ，故半径为  $1$ 。当
 $s>\sigma _0$  时，

$$U_s(1)=\frac1{1-B_s(1)}<\infty,$$

所以其半径至少为  $1$ ；另一方面，一字母词已经给出
 $u_j(s)\ge b_j(s)$ ，故  $U_s$  的半径不可能超过  $B_s$  的半径  $1$ 。于是

$$R_U(s)=
\begin{cases}
r_s,&s<\sigma _0,\\
1,&s\ge\sigma _0.
\end{cases}
\tag{6}$$

字母代价  $3,5$  均出现，因此  $u_j(s)>0$  对所有  $j\ge8$  成立。词连接给出

$$u_{m+n}(s)\ge u_m(s)u_n(s).$$

对这一余有限支持应用移位后的 Fekete 论证，得到真正的极限而非仅有上极限：

$$\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac1j\log u_j(s)
 =-\log R_U(s).
\tag{7}$$

4. 向有限窗的双向指数传递
令

$$H_m(s)=\sum_{j=0}^m u_j(s).$$

修正后的有限窗恒等式直接给出，对每个  $m\ge1$ ，

$$\boxed{\;
H_m(s)\le S_{-s}(m)\le4H_{m+1}(s).
\;}
\tag{8}$$

由 (7) 及  $R_U(s)\le1$ ，

$$\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log H_m(s)
 =-\log R_U(s).$$

再由 (8)，

$$\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_{-s}(m)
 =-\log R_U(s),$$

从而得到全实倾斜公式。
5. 解析性与严格凸性
取  $t>-\sigma _0$ ，并定义字母概率

$$\mu_t(p/q)
 :=q^t e^{-P(t)c(p/q)}.$$

根方程说明其总质量为  $1$ 。由于根严格位于  $B_{-t}$  的收敛圆内，存在指数余量；结合

$$\log q\ll d(p/q),\qquad c(p/q)=2d(p/q)+1,$$

可知任意阶的  $t$ -导数及  $P$ -导数均局部一致收敛。解析隐函数定理因而适用，不涉及任何待验证的算子假设。
记

$$X=\log q,\qquad C=c(p/q).$$

微分根方程得

$$P'(t)=\frac{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}X}{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}C},
\tag{9}$$

以及

$$P''(t)
 =\frac{\mathbb E_{\mu_t}\!\left[(X-P'(t)C)^2\right]}
        {\mathbb E_{\mu_t}C}.
\tag{10}$$

分数  $1/2$  与  $1/3$  分别具有  $(C,q)=(3,2)$  与
 $(5,3)$ ，而

$$\frac{\log2}{3}\ne\frac{\log3}{5}.$$

故 (10) 严格为正，证明实解析性与严格凸性。
6. 临界单侧导数
若

$$\frac pq=[0;a_1,\ldots ,a_r]$$

采用末项  $a_r>1$  的正规连分数约定，则

$$d(p/q)=a_1+\cdots+a_r-1,\qquad
c(p/q)=2(a_1+\cdots+a_r)-1.
\tag{11}$$

Panov–Liehl 的固定分母平均定理给出

$$\frac1{\varphi(q)}
\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
(a_1+\cdots+a_r)
 \sim\frac6{\pi^2}(\log q)^2.$$

该结果及其现代定量形式可见 Aistleitner–Borda–Hauke。因此

$$\sum_{q\ge2}q^{-\sigma _0}
 \sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}c(p/q)
 \ll
 \sum_{q\ge2}q^{1-\sigma _0}(\log q)^2<\infty.
\tag{12}$$

同理，

$$\sum_{q\ge2}\varphi(q)q^{-\sigma _0}\log q<\infty.
\tag{13}$$

故可在 (9) 中令  $t\downarrow-\sigma _0$ ，得到所述有限正右导数。冻结侧恒等于零，左导数为零。因此压力在临界点连续但不可微。
综上，现稿中“非整数  $t>-\beta_*$  的压力存在性仍开放”以及“正压力相的解析严格凸性仍开放”均可删除；其精确替代是上述全实倾斜定理。Weinstein 的自由幺半群参数化、乘法性及最大指标公式仅作为既有输入，参见其原文 Notes on Fibonacci partitions。

在充分小的 s>0 时得到，左尾同理由 s<0 得到。因此
x∈Xn​∣Yn​−v∣<δ​∑​dn​(x)t=exp{n(P(t)+o(1))}.
在该集合上
dn​(x)t≤exp{n(tv+∣t∣δ)},
从而
n→∞liminf​n1​logNn​(v,δ)≥P(t)−tv−∣t∣δ.(1)
把这些状态送入 In​⊔In+1​，并从每条生成轨道保留一个代表。由于每条轨道至多贡献四个源状态，所得生成元集合 Gn​ 满足
∣Gn​∣≥41​Nn​(v,δ),L(g)≤n+1(g∈Gn​).(2)
现固定 0<α<vc​，置
ρ=vα​∈(0,1),nm​=⌊ρm⌋.
当 m 充分大时，L(g)≤nm​+1<m。对每个
g∈Gnm​​，定义
zm​(g)=⎩⎨⎧​[a]g,[a]τg,​m−L(g)=2a,m−L(g)=2a+1.​
于是 zm​(g)∈Im​，并且
R(zm​(g))=R(g).
不同 g 属于不同轨道，故 zm​(g) 两两不同。经有限窗重标号得到两两不同的状态 xm​(g)∈Xm​，满足
dm​(xm​(g))=R(g)=dnm​​(xnm​​(g)).
因此
Ym​(xm​(g))=mnm​​Ynm​​(xnm​​(g)),
从而源状态满足 ∣Ynm​​−v∣<δ 时，
∣Ym​−α∣≤ρδ+o(1).
由 (1)–(2)，对任意 ε>ρδ，
m→∞liminf​m1​logNm​(α,ε)≥ρ(P(t)−tv−∣t∣δ).(3)
先令 δ↓0，再令 t↓tc​。由于
v(t)↓vc​,P(t)↓0,
式 (3) 给出
ε↓0lim​m→∞liminf​m1​logNm​(α,ε)≥t↓tc​lim​α(v(t)P(t)​−t)=σ0​α.(4)
该构造对所有充分大的 m 有效；两条轨道分支精确填补两种奇偶差，故不存在格点或子序列障碍。
反向估计直接来自临界压力。若
∣Ym​−α∣<ε，则
dm​(x)−σ0​≥e−σ0​m(α+ε).
因 P(−σ0​)=0，
S−σ0​​(m)=eo(m),
故
Nm​(α,ε)e−σ0​m(α+ε)≤S−σ0​​(m),
即
