结合 (4)–(5)，得到
ε↓0lim​mliminf​m1​logNm​(α,ε)=ε↓0lim​mlimsup​m1​logNm​(α,ε)=σ0​α.(6)
再由
∣Xm​∣=Fm+2​,m1​logFm+2​⟶logφ,
即得所要求的概率局部渐近。
最后验证凸对偶。对 0≤α≤vc​，若 t≤tc​，则
P(t)=0；若 t≥tc​，凸性及右导数 vc​ 给出
P(t)≥vc​(t−tc​).
因此对所有 t∈R，
tα−P(t)≤tc​α,
且 t=tc​ 时等号成立。故
I(α)=logφ+tc​α=logφ−σ0​α(0≤α≤vc​).
在 (vc​,α+​) 上，每个斜率均由唯一的
t>tc​ 暴露；上述倾斜估计取 n=m 即给出相应局部下界。点
α=0 由两个单纤维状态处理，vc​ 与 α+​ 由有效域内部逼近。有效域外，支撑约束给出 I=∞。由于 Ym​ 最终一致支撑于一个紧区间，指数紧性成立；指数 Chebyshev 上界与上述逐点局部下界经有限覆盖遂给出全部开集和闭集的大偏差界。完整 LDP 得证。

 succeeded in 173ms:
判定
命题成立；更精确地，固定小窗宽 ε 时生成层比例收敛到 (α+ε)/vc​，题设的 α/vc​ 仅在随后令 ε↓0 时出现。
定理（有限窗条件相分离）
设
0<ε<min{α,vc​−α}.
则在题设条件均匀分布下，
(mlogdm​(Xm,α,ε​)​,mL(gm​(Xm,α,ε​))​)Prm→∞​(α+ε,vc​α+ε​).(1)
因此，对任意 η>0，一旦
0<ε<min{α,vc​−α,ηvc​},
即有
m→∞lim​Pr{​mL(gm​)​−vc​α​​>η}=0,
从而题设迭代极限等于 0。
证明
1. 双层重标号与四分支的精确权重
记
Ij​=[Fj+1​−1,Fj+2​−1).
现稿的有限窗对应把 Xm​ 双射到不交并 Im​⊔Im+1​，并保持
dm​(x)=R(n)；两个单点纤维分别对应
Fm+1​−1 与 Fm+2​−1。
依 Weinstein 的自由幺半群分解，非空生成元唯一对应字
w=q1​p1​​×⋯×qr​pr​​,
且现稿接口给出
R(g)=i=1∏r​qi​,L(g)=i=1∑r​c(pi​/qi​).(2)

若 c=L(g)，四个轨道分支及其层成本为
分支[a]g[a]τgσ([a]g)σ([a]τg)​层成本c+2ac+1+2ac+1+2ac+2+2a.​​(3)
中间两支成本相同但由自由作用保证为不同轨道点。偶偏移由第一、第四支填充，奇偏移由第二、第三支填充。因此在单层 Ij​ 中，一个成本为 c 的生成元贡献
κj​(c)=⎩⎨⎧​0,1,2,​j<c,j=c,j>c.​(4)
这同时消除了所有奇偶子序列问题。
在 Im​⊔Im+1​ 上相加，得到精确有限窗权重
Km​(c)=⎩⎨⎧​4,3,1,0,​c≤m−1,c=m,c=m+1,c≥m+2.​(5)
两个层端点均为 R=1 的空字贡献；半开区间约定保证公共边界不重复计数。取 ε<α 后，
dm​>exp(m(α−ε))>2
对充分大 m 恒成立，故两个端点及 dm​=2 的例外全部严格排除，而非仅在渐近意义下忽略。
2. 临界更新测度
令字母表
A={p/q:0<p<q, (p,q)=1},
并采用现稿已经建立的临界概率
ν(p/q)=q−σ0​.(6)
其归一化、有限一阶矩及临界速度识别均作为现稿输入：
μH​:=Eν​[logq]<∞,μC​:=Eν​[c(p/q)]<∞,μC​μH​​=vc​.(7)
设 (Ci​,Hi​) 为独立同分布字母成本
Ci​=c(pi​/qi​),Hi​=logqi​,
并记
Sn​=i=1∑n​Ci​,Tn​=i=1∑n​Hi​,U=n≥1∑​L(Sn​,Tn​).(8)
由于一个字 w 在临界乘积概率下的质量为
ν(w)=e−σ0​H(w),
式 (5) 给出以下精确计数恒等式：对任意位于 t>log2 的 Borel 集 B，
#{x∈Xm​:(L(gm​(x)),logdm​(x))∈B}=∫B​Km​(s)eσ0​tU(ds,dt).(9)
3. 标记更新局部律
置
r=μH​μC​​=vc​1​.
对任意固定 h,δ>0，有
U{t<T≤t+h, ​TS​−r​>δ}⟶0,(10)
以及
U{t<T≤t+h}⟶μH​h​.(11)
式 (11) 是非格点 Blackwell 更新定理的直接应用；log2/log3∈/Q 保证 Hi​ 非格点。可采用 Tsirelson 对 Blackwell 与关键更新定理的统一表述。
式 (10)不需要附加大偏差定理。强大数律给出
Tn​Sn​​⟶r几乎处处.
令
Zt​=n≥1∑​1{t<Tn​≤t+h, ∣Sn​/Tn​−r∣>δ}​.
则 Zt​→0 几乎处处。又因 Hi​≥log2，任何长度为 h 的区间至多包含
⌊log2h​⌋+1
个更新点，故支配收敛定理给出
EZt​→0，即式 (10)。该论证仅使用有限一阶矩，不要求 Ci​ 具有指数矩。
4. 条件集中
写
a=α−ε,b=α+ε<vc​.
选择 δ0​>0 使
(r+δ0​)b<1.(12)
再固定 h>0。由 (10)–(11)，对顶部内区间

有
U{T∈Jm​, ​TS​−r​≤δ0​}=μH​h​+o(1).(13)
式 (12)保证其中所有点满足 S≤m−1，故 Km​(S)=4。条件集合的总基数 Dm,ε​ 因而满足
Dm,ε​≥4eσ0​(bm−2h)(μH​h​+o(1)).(14)
对任意 δ>0，以长度 h 的区间从 bm 向下覆盖 (am,bm)。令
ρm​=t≥am−hsup​U{t<T≤t+h, ​TS​−r​>δ}.
由 (10)，ρm​→0。利用 Km​≤4 及指数权重的几何衰减，
#{x: am<logdm​(x)<bm, ​logdm​(x)L(gm​(x))​−r​>δ}≤1−e−σ0​h4eσ0​bm​ρm​.(15)
除以 (14) 得
logdm​L(gm​)​Pr​vc​1​.(16)
另一方面，对任意 γ>0，每个长度 h 的区间所含更新点数具有一致有界期望，因此
U{am<T≤(b−γ)m}=O(m).
结合 (9) 与 (14)，
Pr{mlogdm​​≤b−γ}=O(me−σ0​γm)⟶0.(17)
条件本身给出 logdm​/m<b，故
mlogdm​​Pr​b.(18)
式 (16)与式 (18)合并即得式 (1)。
极限次序与可加强形式
固定 ε 时，外层极限不可删除：典型生成层比例是
vc​α+ε​,
而非 α/vc​。因此若 0<η<ε/vc​，则题设偏差事件的概率趋于 1。
迭代极限可加强为对角极限。若
εm​↓0,m→∞liminf​mεm​>0,(19)
则存在固定长度的更新子区间始终包含于条件窗内；上述估计一致成立，并给出
mL(gm​(Xm,α,εm​​))​Pr​vc​α​.(20)
特别地，mεm​→∞ 足够，但并非必要。
不能无条件推广到任意 εm​↓0。令
Dm​={logdm​(x):x∈Xm​}.
在任何满足 αm∈/Dm​ 的无限子序列上，取
0<εm​<2m1​dist(αm,Dm​)
即可使条件集合为空。这是明确的收缩窗算术障碍；处理 mεm​→0 需要新的乘数局部定理。
此外，集中一般不具有指数速度。字母 1/q 满足
c(1/q)=2q−1,ν(1/q)=q−σ0​.
取 q≍m 可在仅增加 O(logm) 能量的同时增加 O(m) 生成层成本；把该字母接在典型更新字之前，即产生多项式量级的异常概率。因此严格相分离成立，但生成层上尾保留抛物型重尾。
文献输入与新颖性边界
公开输入仅包括：


Weinstein 的自由幺半群、生成元唯一性、乘数分解及轨道层结构；其论文明确给出自由幺半群识别与最终每层 2Ψ(k) 的计数，Experimental Mathematics 25 (2016), 482–499。


Kesseböhmer–Stratmann 的 Stern–Brocot 压力及多重分形谱，Stochastics and Dynamics 4 (2004), 77–84 与 Crelle 605 (2007), 133–163；本证明未重新推导其结论。


Fiala–Kleban–Özlük 的 Farey 模型相变输入，Journal of Statistical Physics 110 (2003), 73–86；本证明不使用其证明过程。


Blackwell 更新定理及标准更新报酬强律。


现稿既有而非本轮新增的是：双层重标号、四分支成本公式、临界归一化、有限一阶矩识别及 vc​=μH​/μC​。
真正新增的是：


精确微正则计数表示式 (9)；


固定窗联合条件极限 (1)；


题设迭代相分离及对角加强 (20)；


收缩窗算术障碍与抛物字母导致的非指数集中机制。


截至 2026-08-08，arXiv 对 “Fibonacci partitions + conditional limit / microcanonical / phase separation / generator layer / Zeckendorf fibers” 的组合检索未发现相同结论。Weinstein 当前公开引文链所列工作主要涉及均值、方差、幂和、局部动力系统及极值问题，例如 Chow–Slattery、Chow–Jones、Sanna 与 Kempton，均未研究在乘数微正则条件下的生成层比例。zbMATH Open、Crossref/出版商元数据及 Weinstein 引文链亦未检出该有限窗条件定理；MathSciNet 的认证检索不可用，故新颖性结论应表述为“在上述可核验数据库中未发现先例”，而非无条件优先权声明。

判定
统一命题不成立。精确条件 dm​=km​ 保留了 km​ 的素因子结构；临界更新测度所得速度 vc​ 是对全部乘数进行 Dirichlet 加权后的退火速度，不能逐系数转化为任意 km​ 上的淬火速度。
算术局部结构
令
aℓ​(q):=#{1≤p<q:(p,q)=1, c(p/q)=ℓ},Uj​(k):=#{g:R(g)=k, L(g)=j}.
Weinstein 的自由幺半群参数化与现稿加权更新命题给出下述系数级递推：
U0​(k)=1{k=1}​,Uj​(k)=q∣kq≥2​∑​ℓ=1∑j​aℓ​(q)Uj−ℓ​(k/q).(1)
这是既有更新恒等式的 Dirichlet 系数展开，不构成新增结果。它却给出了所需的精确算术判据。若
C(q):={ℓ:aℓ​(q)>0},
则可实现成本集合恰为
J(k)=r≥1q1​⋯qr​=k​⋃​(C(q1​)+⋯+C(qr​)).(2)
因此，logk 不决定局部稠密性；决定因素是 k 的有序因子分解及各因子的连分数成本谱。
对 k>2，相邻双层中的四分支权重精确为
Km​(j)=⎩⎨⎧​4,3,1,0,​j≤m−1,j=m,j=m+1,j≥m+2,​
从而

端点只涉及 k=1；奇偶分支已经完全包含在 4,3,1 三个权重中。
若素因子集合固定为 S，令 νS​(q) 为赋值向量，则联合格点

给出点态局部定理的全部格点障碍。即使投影到 logq 后呈非格点性，精确乘数条件仍固定整个赋值向量，不能用一维非格点更新定理替代。

若成本一阶矩有限且窗口截断具有固定余量，则
mL(gm​)​Pr​vS,μ​α​,vS,μ​=Ez​C∑i​μi​logπi​​.(18)
公式 (17)–(18) 可在鞍点、协方差与截断余量均处于紧集时一致化。素因子集无界或鞍点趋近边界时，不存在统一版本。若进一步要求 Uj​(k) 对单个 j 的点态渐近，还必须另行证明与格点 LS​ 相容的局部极限定理；仅有一阶矩不足以支持该强化。
公开输入、新增结论与新颖性核查
公开输入包括：Weinstein 的自由幺半群、乘数乘法性与轨道分解 Notes on Fibonacci Partitions；固定分母部分商统计 Aistleitner–Borda–Hauke；对整数区间平均的有序因子分解局部统计 Lau 及 Hwang–Janson。递推 (1)、权重 (3) 与公式 (6) 均不应申报为新增。
可独立申报的内容仅为：精确素数幂乘数下的加权生成成本更新桥极限 (9)、有限窗转移 (12)、严格速度分离证书 (10)–(11)、由此得到的统一命题反例，以及有限素因子鞍点版本 (17)–(18)。
截至 2026-08-08：


arXiv API 对 “exact denominator + continued fraction”“ordered factorization + renewal”“multiplicative renewal + local limit” 均无命中；“fixed denominator + partial quotients”仅命中上述固定分母论文。宽检索“ordered factorizations”命中 Lau、Hwang–Janson 等平均型结果，未发现 Weinstein 加权成本的精确乘数条件极限。


Crossref 检索确认相关文献集中于固定因子数、n≤x 平均及固定分母部分商统计；未发现公式 (9)、(12) 或 (17) 的同型结论。


zbMATH Open API 中 Weinstein 条目为 1339.05026；“ordered factorizations”有 38 项，“fixed denominator + partial quotients”有 3 项，“multiplicative renewal + local limit”及“exact conditioning + continued fraction”均无命中。


Weinstein 引文链检得 Weinstein 2020、Chow–Slattery、Shallit、Chow–Jones、Sanna 与 Kempton；其主题分别为行列式、精确表示公式、均值、方差、整数矩及动力学局部结构，均未处理精确乘数下的生成成本条件极限。


MathSciNet 的公共入口未返回可独立核验的检索记录，故不能宣称完成其订阅数据库内部的穷尽检索；该项是当前新颖性核查的唯一数据库缺口。


因此，原统一命题应予反驳；可发表的正确结论是“精确乘数条件产生由素因子赋值方向决定的淬火速度”，而非临界退火速度 vc​ 的普适继承。

ID math/0307150
TITLE: arXiv Query: search_query=&amp;id_list=math/0307150&amp;start=0&amp;max_results=10
TITLE: Notes on Fibonacci Partitions
SUMMARY: Let $f_1=1,f_2=2$ and $f_i=f_{i-1}+f_{i-2}$ for $i&gt;2$ be the sequence of Fibonacci numbers. Let $Φ_h(n)$ be the quantity of partitions of natural number $n$ into $h$ different Fibonacci numbers. In terms of Zeckendorf partition of $n$ I deduce a formula for the function $Φ(n;t):=\sum_{h\geq 1}Φ_h(n)t^h$, and use it to analyze the functions $F(n):=Φ(n;1)$ and $χ(n):=Φ(n;-1)$. I obtain the least upper bound for $F(n)$ when $f_i-1\&lt;n\&lt;f_{i+1}-1$. It implies that $F(n)\&lt;\sqrt{n+1}$ for any natural $n$. I prove also that $|χ(n)|\&lt;1$, and $\mathop{\lim}\limits_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N} \left(χ^2(1)+χ^2(2)\dptχ^2(N)\right)=0$. For any $k\&gt;2$, I define a special finite set $\mathbb{G}(k)$ of solutions of the equation $F(n)=k$, all solutions can be easily obtained from $\mathbb{G}(k)$. This construction uses a representation of rational numbers as certain continued fractions and provides with a canonical identification $\coprod_{k\&gt;2}\mathbb{G}(k)=\G_+$, where $\G_+$ is the monoid freely generated by the positive rational numbers $&lt;1$. Let $Ψ(k)$ be the cardinality of $\mathbb{G}(k)$. I prove that, for $i\&gt;2k$ and $k\&gt;2$, the interval $[f_i-1,f_{i+1}-1]$ contains exactly $2Ψ(k)$ solutions of the equation $F(n)=k$ and offer a formula for the Dirichlet generating function of the sequence $Ψ(k)$. I formulate conjectures on the set of minimal solutions of the equations $F(n)=k$ as $k$ varies and pose some questions concerning such solutions.
ID 2311.06006
TITLE: arXiv Query: search_query=&amp;id_list=2311.06006&amp;start=0&amp;max_results=10
TITLE: The Dynamics of the Fibonacci Partition Function
SUMMARY: In this article we study the local structure of the Fibonacci Partition Function by relating it to a cocycle over an irrational rotation.
ID 2309.12724
TITLE: arXiv Query: search_query=&amp;id_list=2309.12724&amp;start=0&amp;max_results=10
TITLE: A note on the power sums of the number of Fibonacci partitions
SUMMARY: For every nonnegative integer $n$, let $r_F(n)$ be the number of ways to write $n$ as a sum of Fibonacci numbers, where the order of the summands does not matter. Moreover, for all positive integers $p$ and $N$, let \begin{equation*} S_{F}^{(p)}(N) := \sum_{n = 0}^{N - 1} \big(r_F(n)\big)^p . \end{equation*} Chow, Jones, and Slattery determined the order of growth of $S_{F}^{(p)}(N)$ for $p \in \{1,2\}$. We prove that, for all positive integers $p$, there exists a real number $λ_p &gt; 1$ such that \begin{equation*} S^{(p)}_F(N) \asymp_p N^{(\log λ_p) /\!\log \varphi} \end{equation*} as $N \to +\infty$, where $\varphi := (1 + \sqrt{5})/2$ is the golden ratio. Furthermore, we show that \begin{equation*} \lim_{p \to +\infty} λ_p^{1/p} = \varphi^{1/2} . \end{equation*} Our proofs employ automata theory and a result on the generalized spectral radius due to Blondel and Nesterov.

codex
The strongest claim appears to be Theorem E / `thm:all-real-pressure`: exact pressure for every real tilt, analytic strict convexity above the critical point, and a first-order freezing corner at \(t=-\sigma_0\). The full LDP is a meaningful corollary-strength extension, but the pressure theorem is the cleaner headline because it identifies the phase transition exactly and drives the later results.

id: http://arxiv.org/abs/math/0509603v2
title: A multifractal analysis for Stern-Brocot intervals, continued fractions and Diophantine growth rates
summary: In this paper we obtain multifractal generalizations of classical results by Lévy and Khintchin in metrical Diophantine approximations and measure theory of continued fractions. We give a complete multifractal analysis for Stern--Brocot intervals, for continued fractions and for certain Diophantine growth rates. In particular, we give detailed discussions of two multifractal spectra closely related to the Farey map and the Gauss map.

\subsection{One-sided thick-fiber envelopes}

\begin{definition}[Exponential thick fibers]
\label{def:thick-fibers}
For \(\alpha\ge0\), set
\[
  \mathcal N_m(\alpha):=
  \#\left\{x\in X_m:\frac1m\log d_m(x)\ge\alpha\right\}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{corollary}[Discrete pressure upper envelope for thick fibers]
\label{cor:thick-fiber-envelope}
\leanverified{paper_projection_thick_fiber_envelope}
For every \(\alpha\ge 0\),
\[
  \limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\log \mathcal N_m(\alpha)
  \le
  \min\left\{
    \log\varphi,\,
    \inf_{q\ge 1}\bigl(\log \lambda_q-q\alpha\bigr)
  \right\},
\]
where the infimum runs over positive integers \(q\).  Setting
\[
  \alpha_\ast:=\frac12\log\varphi,
\]
this barrier is sharp in the sense that
\[
  \alpha>\alpha_\ast
  \Longrightarrow
  \mathcal N_m(\alpha)=0 \text{ for all sufficiently large }m,
\]
whereas
\[
  0\le \alpha<\alpha_\ast
  \Longrightarrow
  \mathcal N_m(\alpha)\ge 1 \text{ for all sufficiently large }m.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(\alpha\ge 0\), and let \(q\ge 1\) be an integer.  Every
\(x\in X_m\) counted by \(\mathcal N_m(\alpha)\) contributes at least
\(e^{q\alpha m}\) to \(S_q(m)\), so
\[
  e^{q\alpha m}\mathcal N_m(\alpha)
  \le
  \sum_{\substack{x\in X_m\\ d_m(x)\ge e^{\alpha m}}} d_m(x)^q
  \le
  S_q(m).
\]
Hence
\[
  \limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\log \mathcal N_m(\alpha)
  \le
  \log\lambda_q-q\alpha
\]
by \Cref{thm:all-q-transfer}.  Taking the infimum over \(q\ge 1\) gives the
pressure bound.  Since \(\mathcal N_m(\alpha)\le \card{X_m}=F_{m+2}\), we also
have
\[
  \limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\log \mathcal N_m(\alpha)\le \log\varphi.
\]

For the threshold statement, \Cref{thm:max-fiber} gives
\(
\frac{1}{m}\log D_m\to \alpha_\ast.
\)
If \(\alpha>\alpha_\ast\), then eventually \(D_m<e^{\alpha m}\), so
\(\mathcal N_m(\alpha)=0\).  If \(0\le \alpha<\alpha_\ast\), then eventually
\(D_m\ge e^{\alpha m}\), so at least one fiber is counted.
\end{proof}

\section{A confluent rewriting model}\label{sec:rewriting}

We consider the operator words generated by normalization and conditional
expectations.  This fragment is exact and confluent.  When additional
operators are introduced, exact equality is too rigid, and operator-norm error
provides a natural replacement.

\subsection{An exact rewriting system}

\begin{definition}[Exact rewrite system]
\label{def:rewrite-system}
Let \(\mathcal R\) be the rewriting system on the free monoid
\(\mathcal W_{Z,E}\) generated by the rules
\[
  ZZ\longrightarrow Z,
  \qquad
  E_mE_n\longrightarrow E_{\min\{m,n\}}
  \qquad (m,n\ge 1).
\]
We write \(W\to_{\mathcal R}W'\) for a one-step reduction and
\(\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal R}^*\) for the congruence generated by these
reductions.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:rewrite-semantics}
The rule \(ZZ\to Z\) expresses idempotence of normalization.  The rule
\(E_mE_n\to E_{\min\{m,n\}}\) is the tower property of conditional expectation
along nested visible \(\sigma\)-algebras.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Termination]
\label{lem:rewrite-termination}
The system \(\mathcal R\) is terminating.
\leanverified{paper_projection_rewrite_termination}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Every reduction shortens the word length by one, so an infinite reduction
sequence is impossible.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local confluence]
\label{lem:rewrite-local-confluence}
The system \(\mathcal R\) is locally confluent.
\leanverified{paper_projection_rewrite_local_confluence_seeds}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The only critical overlaps occur in words of length three.

For \(ZZZ\), reducing the first or the last pair leads to the same intermediate
word \(ZZ\), hence to \(Z\).

For \(E_aE_bE_c\), reducing the first pair gives
\[
  E_{\min\{a,b\}}E_c\to E_{\min\{a,b,c\}},
\]
while reducing the second pair gives
\[
  E_aE_{\min\{b,c\}}\to E_{\min\{a,b,c\}}.
\]
There are no mixed critical pairs because the left-hand sides of the two rules
involve disjoint alphabets.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Termination and confluence]
\label{thm:rewrite-confluence}
The exact rewrite system \(\mathcal R\) is terminating and confluent.
Consequently every word \(W\in\mathcal W_{Z,E}\) has a unique irreducible
normal form \(\mathrm{NF}(W)\).
\leanverified{paper_projection_rewrite_confluence_seeds}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Combine \Cref{lem:rewrite-termination,lem:rewrite-local-confluence} with
Newman's lemma.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Normal forms and decidability]
\label{cor:rewrite-word-problem}
A word is irreducible if and only if it contains no subword \(ZZ\) and no
adjacent pair \(E_mE_n\).  Equivalently, every normal form is an alternating
word
\[
  E_{m_0}ZE_{m_1}Z\cdots ZE_{m_r},
\]
with either endpoint \(E\)-letter allowed to be absent.  Moreover,
\[
  W_1\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal R}^* W_2
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \mathrm{NF}(W_1)=\mathrm{NF}(W_2),
\]
so the exact word problem is decidable in time linear in the total word length.
\leanverified{paper_projection_rewrite_word_problem_seeds}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Irreducibility is immediate from the form of the rules.  In a terminating and
confluent system two words are equivalent if and only if their normal forms
agree.  The normal form is obtained by collapsing each maximal \(Z\)-block to a
single \(Z\) and each maximal \(E\)-block to one letter indexed by the minimum
visible level appearing in that block.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Linear-time reduction algorithm]
\label{prop:rewrite-algorithm}
There is a deterministic linear-time procedure that maps every
\(W\in\mathcal W_{Z,E}\) to its normal form and simultaneously produces a
certificate consisting of exactly
\[
  \ell(W)-\ell(\mathrm{NF}(W))
\]
elementary contractions, where \(\ell(\cdot)\) denotes word length.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Scan the word from left to right.  Replace each maximal run of \(Z\)'s by one
copy of \(Z\), and replace each maximal run \(E_{m_1}\cdots E_{m_s}\) by the
single letter \(E_{\min\{m_1,\dots,m_s\}}\).  Every discarded letter records
one elementary contraction, and no backtracking is required.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Metric comparison beyond the exact system}

The exact confluence theorem applies only to the operator words above.  Once
additional operators are introduced, the relevant notion of comparison is small
semantic error rather than literal equality of words.

\begin{definition}[\texorpdfstring{$\varepsilon$}{epsilon}-sound rewrites]
\label{def:epsilon-sound}
Let \(W\) and \(W'\) be operator words with the same source and target state
simplices, and write \(\mathrm{Sem}(W)\) for the affine map represented by
\(W\).  We call a rewrite \(W\Rightarrow_{\varepsilon} W'\)
\emph{\(\varepsilon\)-sound} if
\[
  \|\mathrm{Sem}(W)-\mathrm{Sem}(W')\|_{1\to 1}\le \varepsilon.
\]
Exact rewrites are therefore \(0\)-sound.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Lawvere-type error calculus]
\label{prop:lawvere-distance}
If \(W\Rightarrow_{\varepsilon} W'\) and \(W'\Rightarrow_{\delta} W''\), then
\(W\Rightarrow_{\varepsilon+\delta} W''\).  Consequently the quantity
\[
  \begin{aligned}
  d_L(W,W')
  &:=\inf\Bigl\{\sum_{j=1}^N \varepsilon_j:
  W=W_0\Rightarrow_{\varepsilon_1}W_1\Rightarrow_{\varepsilon_2}\cdots \\
  &\hspace{5em}
  \Rightarrow_{\varepsilon_N}W_N=W'\Bigr\}
  \end{aligned}
\]
is a pseudo-metric on operator words with fixed source and target.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The triangle inequality for the operator norm gives
\[
  \|\mathrm{Sem}(W)-\mathrm{Sem}(W'')\|_{1\to1}
  \le
  \|\mathrm{Sem}(W)-\mathrm{Sem}(W')\|_{1\to1}
  +
  \|\mathrm{Sem}(W')-\mathrm{Sem}(W'')\|_{1\to1}
  \le \varepsilon+\delta.
\]
The pseudo-metric properties of \(d_L\) follow by taking infima over zigzags.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Boundary of the exact theory]
\label{rem:rewrite-boundary}
The confluence theorem is specific to the exact system above.  More elaborate
operator languages should be compared in the pseudo-metric of
\Cref{prop:lawvere-distance}.  The finite-state kernels of
\Cref{sec:moment-kernel} are exact after compilation into matrix realizations,
but not necessarily at the level of naive word reduction.
\end{remark}

\section{Visible entropy rates and canonical bands}\label{sec:ledger}

Since \(p_m(x)=d_m(x)/2^m\), the visible law under uniform input is the
\(q=1\) canonical ensemble.  With the all-\(q\) asymptotics of \(S_q(m)\) in
hand, we obtain its integer-order R\'enyi entropy rates
\cite{CoverThomas2006} and sharp exponential tail estimates.

\begin{definition}[Visible law under uniform input]
\label{def:visible-law}
For \(m\ge0\), the visible law is the probability measure on \(X_m\) obtained
by pushing the uniform law on \(\Omega_m\) forward by \(\Fold_m\):
\[
  p_m(x):=2^{-m}\card{\{\omega\in\Omega_m:\Fold_m(\omega)=x\}}
  =
  \frac{d_m(x)}{2^m}
  \qquad (x\in X_m).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{corollary}[Typical visible thickness band]
\label{cor:visible-band}
For every \(\varepsilon>0\), let
\[
  B_m(\varepsilon):=
  \left\{
    x\in X_m:
    \Delta_1-\varepsilon\le \frac1m\log d_m(x)
    \le \Delta_2+\varepsilon
  \right\}.
\]
Then
\[
  p_m(B_m(\varepsilon))
  \ge 1-\exp\!\bigl(-(\varepsilon+o(1))m\bigr).
\]
Here \(\Delta_1=\log2-\log\varphi\), because
\(S_1(m)=2^m\) and \(S_0(m)=F_{m+2}\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:visible-law},
\[
  p_m(x)=\frac{d_m(x)}{S_1(m)}=\mu_{m,1}(x).
\]
Apply \Cref{thm:gibbs-selection} with \(q=1\).  The two exceptional tails
outside the displayed band each have mass at most
\(\exp(-(\varepsilon+o(1))m)\), and the sum of two such errors is still
\(\exp(-(\varepsilon+o(1))m)\), giving the displayed lower bound for the
band mass.  Finally,
\[
  \Delta_1=P_1-P_0=\log2-\log\varphi,
\]
using \(P_1=\log\lambda_1=\log2\) from \(S_1(m)=2^m\) and
\(P_0=\log\varphi\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Integer-order R\'enyi entropy rates]
\label{thm:renyi-entropy-rate}
\leanverified{paper_projection_renyi_entropy_rate}
For every integer \(q\ge2\), let
\[
  H_q(p_m):=\frac{1}{1-q}\log\sum_{x\in X_m}p_m(x)^q
\]
be the R\'enyi entropy of order \(q\).  Then the entropy rate
\[
  h_q:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1mH_q(p_m)
\]
exists and is
\[
  h_q=\frac{q\log2-P_q}{q-1}.
\]
Moreover,
\[
  \lim_{q\to\infty}h_q=\log2-\frac12\log\varphi .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:visible-law},
\[
  \sum_{x\in X_m}p_m(x)^q
  =
  2^{-mq}\sum_{x\in X_m}d_m(x)^q
  =
  2^{-mq}S_q(m).
\]
Therefore
\[
  H_q(p_m)
  =
  \frac{1}{1-q}\bigl(\log S_q(m)-mq\log2\bigr)
  =
  \frac{mq\log2-\log S_q(m)}{q-1}.
\]
For fixed \(q\ge2\), \Cref{thm:all-q-transfer} gives
\[
  \frac1m\log S_q(m)\to P_q,
\]
so the displayed rate follows.  Finally,
\[
  h_q
  =
  \frac{q}{q-1}\log2-\frac{q}{q-1}\cdot\frac{P_q}{q}.
\]
By \Cref{cor:renyi-endpoint}, \(P_q/q=\log(\lambda_q^{1/q})\to
\frac12\log\varphi\), and hence \(h_q\to
\log2-\frac12\log\varphi\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Visible-tail envelope for thick fibers]
\label{cor:visible-thick-tail}
\leanverified{paper_projection_visible_thick_tail}
For \(\alpha\ge0\), set
\[
  E_m(\alpha):=
  \left\{x\in X_m:\frac1m\log d_m(x)\ge\alpha\right\}.
\]
Then, for every integer \(q\ge1\),
\[
  \limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log p_m(E_m(\alpha))
  \le
  P_q-\log2-(q-1)\alpha .
\]
Consequently,
\[
  \limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log p_m(E_m(\alpha))
  \le
  \inf_{q\ge1}\bigl(P_q-\log2-(q-1)\alpha\bigr).
\]
If \(\alpha>\frac12\log\varphi\), then \(p_m(E_m(\alpha))=0\) for all
sufficiently large \(m\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
On \(E_m(\alpha)\), the inequality \(d_m(x)\ge e^{\alpha m}\) gives
\[
  d_m(x)\le e^{-(q-1)\alpha m}d_m(x)^q
  \qquad(q\ge1).
\]
Thus
\[
  p_m(E_m(\alpha))
  =
  2^{-m}\sum_{x\in E_m(\alpha)}d_m(x)
  \le
  2^{-m}e^{-(q-1)\alpha m}S_q(m).
\]
Taking logarithms, dividing by \(m\), and applying
\Cref{thm:all-q-transfer} gives the first bound.  Taking the infimum over
\(q\ge1\) gives the second.  The final assertion follows from
\Cref{thm:max-fiber}: if \(\alpha>\frac12\log\varphi\), then eventually
\(D_m<e^{\alpha m}\), so \(E_m(\alpha)=\varnothing\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Collision entropy rate]
\label{thm:collision-entropy-rate}
\leanverified{paper_projection_collision_entropy_rate}
The collision entropy
\[
  H_2(p_m):=-\log\sum_{x\in X_m}p_m(x)^2
\]
satisfies
\[
  \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1mH_2(p_m)=2\log2-P_2.
\]
Equivalently, \(H_2(p_m)=h_2m+o(m)\) with
\[
  h_2=2\log2-\log\lambda_2.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
This is \Cref{thm:renyi-entropy-rate} with \(q=2\).  Explicitly,
\[
  \sum_{x\in X_m}p_m(x)^2=2^{-2m}S_2(m),
\]
so
\[
  H_2(p_m)=2m\log2-\log S_2(m).
\]
Since \(m^{-1}\log S_2(m)\to P_2=\log\lambda_2\), the asserted rate follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[R\'enyi-2 asymptotics]
\label{cor:renyi2}
Let \(p(\lambda)=\lambda^3-2\lambda^2-2\lambda+2\), and let
\(\lambda_-\in(-2,-1)\), \(\lambda_+\in(0,1)\), and \(\rho\in(2,3)\) be its
three real roots.  With the constants
\[
  T_2(m)=c_\rho\rho^m+c_-\lambda_-^m+c_+\lambda_+^m
\]
from \Cref{thm:q2-alternating-second-order}, one has
\[
  \sum_{x\in X_m}p_m(x)^2
  =
  4^{-m}\bigl(c_\rho\rho^m+c_-\lambda_-^m+c_+\lambda_+^m\bigr),
\]
where \(c_\rho>0\) and \(c_-\ne0\).  In particular,
\[
  \begin{aligned}
  H_2(p_m)
  &=
  m(2\log2-\log\rho)-\log c_\rho\\
  &\quad{}
  -\log\!\left(
    1+\frac{c_-}{c_\rho}\left(\frac{\lambda_-}{\rho}\right)^m
      +\frac{c_+}{c_\rho}\left(\frac{\lambda_+}{\rho}\right)^m
  \right),
  \end{aligned}
\]
and the leading rate is \(2\log2-\log\rho=2\log2-P_2\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:T2}, \(T_2(m)=S_2(m)\).  The exact expansion of \(T_2(m)\) is
\Cref{thm:q2-alternating-second-order}; substituting it into
\[
  \sum_{x\in X_m}p_m(x)^2=4^{-m}S_2(m)
\]
gives the first display.  Since \(\rho\) is the unique dominant root and
\(c_\rho>0\), factor \(c_\rho\rho^m\) out of \(S_2(m)\) and take minus the
logarithm.  This gives the displayed formula for \(H_2(p_m)\).
\Cref{rem:chow-jones} identifies the dominant root \(\rho\) with
\(\lambda_2\), so \(\log\rho=P_2\).
\end{proof}

1. **HEADLINE RESULT.** **Theorem E (`thm:all-real-pressure`)**: the pressure
\[
P(t)=\lim_{m\to\infty}m^{-1}\log\sum_{x\in X_m}d_m(x)^t
\]
exists for every \(t\in\mathbb R\), is zero exactly for \(t\le-\sigma_0\), is the unique positive solution of the stated weighted-generator equation for \(t>-\sigma_0\), is analytic and strictly convex there, and has \(P'_-(-\sigma_0)=0<P'_+(-\sigma_0)\).

2. **IS IT ACTUALLY NEW? — INCREMENTAL.** The nearest prior work is Weinstein, *Notes on Fibonacci Partitions* (2016), which already supplies the free generator monoid, multiplicative weights, orbit decomposition, stabilization, and Dirichlet series, together with Kesseböhmer–Stratmann’s Stern–Brocot pressure theory; Kempton’s *The Dynamics of the Fibonacci Partition Function* is the nearest recent comparator. No searched source states this finite-window pressure law or LDP, but the headline theorem is a specialized weighted-renewal transfer of those existing structures, not a fundamentally new framework.

3. **DEPTH OF THE MATHEMATICS.** Moderately substantial: the parity-complete orbit-filling argument for nonexposed LDP slopes is a genuine mechanism, but most of the pressure, Gibbs, Tauberian, and conditional results become standard renewal/convex-analysis deductions once Weinstein’s parametrization is imported; the numerical speed certificate is largely bookkeeping.

4. **TOP-JOURNAL VERDICT. — TIER-3.** This is a solid specialist contribution if the central imported-pressure step is fully documented, but it is too model-specific and too dependent on existing Weinstein/Stern–Brocot machinery for TAMS/ETDS level as written.

5. **WHAT WOULD MOVE IT UP ONE TIER.** Prove a uniform multivariate local theorem for every fixed prime set \(S\): for exponent rays \(r^{(m)}/m\to\theta\) in the admissible interior, obtain
\[
C_m\!\left(\prod_{p\in S}p^{r_p^{(m)}}\right)\sim4A(\theta)m^{-(|S|-1)/2}e^{mh(\theta)}
\]
with explicit \(A,h\), and a conditional CLT \((L(g_m)-m\ell(\theta))/\sqrt m\Rightarrow N(0,\sigma^2(\theta))\), uniformly under a positive layer margin.

6. **WEAKEST LINK.** The claim \(a_d(s)\asymp_s e^{d\mathfrak P(s/2)}\) is attributed generically to Stern–Brocot/Knauf literature without an exact theorem citation or a proof matching the manuscript’s depth, \(s/2\), and “new-denominator layer” normalization; it supports the radius calculation and hence the all-real pressure theorem, while the passing verification scripts test only finite identities and cannot certify this asymptotic.

1. **HEADLINE RESULT.** **Theorem E (`thm:all-real-pressure`)**: the pressure
\[
P(t)=\lim_{m\to\infty}m^{-1}\log\sum_{x\in X_m}d_m(x)^t
\]
exists for every \(t\in\mathbb R\), is zero exactly for \(t\le-\sigma_0\), is the unique positive solution of the stated weighted-generator equation for \(t>-\sigma_0\), is analytic and strictly convex there, and has \(P'_-(-\sigma_0)=0<P'_+(-\sigma_0)\).

2. **IS IT ACTUALLY NEW? — INCREMENTAL.** The nearest prior work is Weinstein, *Notes on Fibonacci Partitions* (2016), which already supplies the free generator monoid, multiplicative weights, orbit decomposition, stabilization, and Dirichlet series, together with Kesseböhmer–Stratmann’s Stern–Brocot pressure theory; Kempton’s *The Dynamics of the Fibonacci Partition Function* is the nearest recent comparator. No searched source states this finite-window pressure law or LDP, but the headline theorem is a specialized weighted-renewal transfer of those existing structures, not a fundamentally new framework.

3. **DEPTH OF THE MATHEMATICS.** Moderately substantial: the parity-complete orbit-filling argument for nonexposed LDP slopes is a genuine mechanism, but most of the pressure, Gibbs, Tauberian, and conditional results become standard renewal/convex-analysis deductions once Weinstein’s parametrization is imported; the numerical speed certificate is largely bookkeeping.

4. **TOP-JOURNAL VERDICT. — TIER-3.** This is a solid specialist contribution if the central imported-pressure step is fully documented, but it is too model-specific and too dependent on existing Weinstein/Stern–Brocot machinery for TAMS/ETDS level as written.

5. **WHAT WOULD MOVE IT UP ONE TIER.** Prove a uniform multivariate local theorem for every fixed prime set \(S\): for exponent rays \(r^{(m)}/m\to\theta\) in the admissible interior, obtain
\[
C_m\!\left(\prod_{p\in S}p^{r_p^{(m)}}\right)\sim4A(\theta)m^{-(|S|-1)/2}e^{mh(\theta)}
\]
with explicit \(A,h\), and a conditional CLT \((L(g_m)-m\ell(\theta))/\sqrt m\Rightarrow N(0,\sigma^2(\theta))\), uniformly under a positive layer margin.

6. **WEAKEST LINK.** The claim \(a_d(s)\asymp_s e^{d\mathfrak P(s/2)}\) is attributed generically to Stern–Brocot/Knauf literature without an exact theorem citation or a proof matching the manuscript’s depth, \(s/2\), and “new-denominator layer” normalization; it supports the radius calculation and hence the all-real pressure theorem, while the passing verification scripts test only finite identities and cannot certify this asymptotic.
